  "short_description": {
    "id": "Graoully adalah naga legenda Metz yang tinggal di amfiteater Romawi dan meracuni kota sampai dikalahkan Santo Klemens, uskup pertama Metz.",
    "en": "Graoully is a legendary dragon of Metz that lived in the Roman amphitheatre and poisoned the city until Saint Clement, its first bishop, overcame it.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Graoully adalah naga dalam cerita rakyat Metz yang tinggal di amfiteater Romawi bersama banyak ular; napas berbisa mereka membuat kota tak bisa dimasuki. Santo Klemens mengangkat salib, mengikat Graoully dengan stolanya, dan menuntunnya ke Sungai Seille, tempat menurut satu versi monster itu ditelan bumi; versi lain menyebut Klemens menusuknya dengan tombak. Versi pertama legenda ini dari akhir abad ke-10 dan melambangkan kemenangan Kristen atas paganisme.",
      "en": "Graoully is a dragon of Metz folklore that lived in the Roman amphitheatre with many serpents whose venomous breath sealed off the city. Saint Clement raised his cross, bound Graoully with his stole and led it to the River Seille, where in one version the earth swallowed it; other versions have him spear it. The legend's first version dates from the late 10th century and symbolises Christianity's victory over paganism.",

      "id": "Namanya mungkin dari grouiller, \"berkerumun\", atau dari bahasa Jerman gräulich, \"mengerikan\". Patungnya diarak di Metz sampai abad ke-19, nama Rue Taison dikaitkan dengan peringatan tentangnya, dan ia menjadi lambang kota serta klub FC Metz sampai 2021.",
      "en": "Its name may come from grouiller, 'to swarm', or German gräulich, 'horrible'. Its effigy was paraded in Metz until the 19th century, the Rue Taison is linked to a warning about it, and it became a city symbol and the FC Metz emblem until 2021.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "graoully-c01",
      "source_id": "graoully-s1",
      "quote": "In French folklore, Graoully (spelled as Graouli, Graouilly, Graouille or Graully) is a creature with the appearance of a dragon. According to legend, it lived in the arena of the Roman amphitheater in Metz, France. Legends state that Saint Clement of Metz fought against Graoully and vanquished the beast.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Prancis, Graoully (juga Graouli, Graouilly, Graouille, atau Graully) adalah makhluk berwujud naga yang menurut legenda tinggal di arena amfiteater Romawi di Metz, dan Santo Klemens dari Metz melawan serta mengalahkannya.",
        "en": "In French folklore Graoully (also Graouli, Graouilly, Graouille or Graully) is a dragon-like creature that by legend lived in the arena of the Roman amphitheatre at Metz, and Saint Clement of Metz fought and overcame it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "graoully-c02",
      "source_id": "graoully-s3",
      "quote": "Mais des serpents installés dans l'amphithéâtre empoisonnent l'air de leur souffle venimeux et interdisent ainsi l'accès à la ville.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ular-ular di amfiteater meracuni udara dengan napas berbisa sehingga orang tidak bisa memasuki kota.",
        "en": "Serpents in the amphitheatre poisoned the air with their venomous breath and so barred access to the city."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "graoully-c03",
      "source_id": "graoully-s1",
      "quote": "The bishop went to the serpents pit, held up his cross and captured Le Graoully with his stole – the long wide band worn over the shoulders. He then led the dragon away and made sure it disappeared over the Seille River along with the rest of the serpents.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sang uskup pergi ke sarang ular, mengangkat salibnya, menangkap Graoully dengan stolanya, lalu menuntun naga itu pergi hingga lenyap di seberang Sungai Seille bersama ular-ular lainnya.",
        "en": "The bishop went to the serpents' pit, raised his cross, caught Graoully with his stole, and led the dragon away until it vanished across the River Seille with the other serpents."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "graoully-c04",
      "source_id": "graoully-s1",
      "quote": "However, in many depictions and versions of the story, Saint Clément instead slew the dragon by piercing it with a polearm.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namun dalam banyak gambaran dan versi, Santo Klemens justru membunuh naga itu dengan menusuknya memakai tombak bergagang panjang.",
        "en": "In many depictions and versions, however, Saint Clement instead killed the dragon by piercing it with a polearm."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "graoully-c05",
      "source_id": "graoully-s2",
      "quote": "Dort wurde das Ungeheuer vom Erdboden verschluckt. Clemens verschloss das Loch mit einem Felsen, der angeblich noch heute zu sehen ist.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Jerman, di sebuah pulau di Seille monster itu ditelan bumi, dan Klemens menutup lubangnya dengan batu yang konon masih terlihat sampai kini.",
        "en": "According to the German article, on an island in the Seille the monster was swallowed by the earth, and Clement sealed the hole with a rock said still to be visible."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "graoully-c06",
      "source_id": "graoully-s1",
      "quote": "It is believed that the word Graoully derives from the French word \"grouiller\" meaning \"swarm\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata Graoully diyakini berasal dari kata Prancis grouiller, \"berkerumun\".",
        "en": "The word Graoully is believed to come from French grouiller, 'to swarm'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "graoully-c07",
      "source_id": "graoully-s2",
      "quote": "Der Name soll sich von „gräulich“ (schrecklich) ableiten.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Jerman menyebut namanya mungkin dari kata gräulich, \"mengerikan\".",
        "en": "The German article says the name may come from gräulich, 'horrible'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "graoully-c08",
      "source_id": "graoully-s3",
      "quote": "La première version de la légende de Saint Clément de Metz date de la fin du Xe siècle.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi pertama legenda Santo Klemens dari Metz berasal dari akhir abad ke-10.",
        "en": "The first version of the legend of Saint Clement of Metz dates from the late 10th century."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "graoully-c09",
      "source_id": "graoully-s3",
      "quote": "Cette légende représente symboliquement la destruction des religions païennes — traditionnellement représentées par la figure du serpent et du dragon — et leur remplacement par le christianisme.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda ini melambangkan hancurnya agama pagan, yang biasa digambarkan sebagai ular dan naga, dan penggantiannya oleh agama Kristen.",
        "en": "The legend symbolises the destruction of pagan religions, traditionally shown as serpent and dragon, and their replacement by Christianity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "graoully-c10",
      "source_id": "graoully-s1",
      "quote": "A procession with an effigy of the dragon was held in the city until the nineteenth century.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Prosesi dengan patung naga itu diadakan di kota sampai abad ke-19.",
        "en": "A procession with an effigy of the dragon was held in the city until the 19th century."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "graoully-c11",
      "source_id": "graoully-s2",
      "quote": "Der Name der Rue Taison, einer Straße unweit der Kathedrale, wird auf eine Warnung vor dem Graoully zurückgeführt: „Taisons, taisons nous, voilà le Graoully qui passe“",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Rue Taison dekat katedral konon berasal dari peringatan tentang Graoully: \"Taisons, taisons nous, voilà le Graoully qui passe\" (diamlah, Graoully sedang lewat).",
        "en": "The name of the Rue Taison near the cathedral is traced to a warning about Graoully: 'Taisons, taisons nous, voilà le Graoully qui passe' (hush, the Graoully is passing)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "graoully-c12",
      "source_id": "graoully-s2",
      "quote": "Eine Figur des Graoully befindet sich in der Krypta der Kathedrale; eine Kopie davon hängt in einem Saal der Hohkönigsburg. Bis 2021 war das Fabeltier auch auf dem Wappen des Fußballclubs FC Metz zu sehen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Patung Graoully ada di kripta katedral Metz, salinannya tergantung di Hohkönigsburg, dan sampai 2021 makhluk ini muncul di lambang klub sepak bola FC Metz.",
        "en": "A figure of Graoully is in the cathedral crypt, a copy hangs in a hall of the Hohkönigsburg, and until 2021 the beast featured on the crest of FC Metz."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Herensuge adalah naga atau ular raksasa mitologi Basque, sering berkepala tujuh, yang tinggal di gua dan menuntut korban sampai dikalahkan Santo Mikael atau seorang pahlawan.",
    "en": "Herensuge is the dragon or giant serpent of Basque mythology, often seven-headed, that lives in caves and demands victims until Saint Michael or a hero defeats it.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "herensuge-c01",
      "herensuge-c04",
      "herensuge-c06",
      "herensuge-c10"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Herensuge adalah naga mitologi Basque yang sering berwujud ular; namanya beragam tetapi selalu berakhiran suge, \"ular\". Ia kadang berkepala tujuh, yang dipercaya anak-anaknya sendiri, dan menurut sebagian sumber dapat terbang. Ia menghuni gua, gunung, dan jurang, menarik manusia dan hewan ke guanya dengan napasnya, dan meneror gembala.",
      "en": "Herensuge is the Basque dragon, often in serpent form; its names vary but always end in suge, 'snake'. It sometimes has seven heads, thought to be its own offspring, and some sources say it can fly. It lives in caves, mountains and chasms, draws people and animals into its lair with its breath, and terrorises shepherds.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "herensuge-c01",
        "herensuge-c02",
        "herensuge-c03",
        "herensuge-c04",
        "herensuge-c05",
        "herensuge-c06",
        "herensuge-c07",
        "herensuge-c08"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam legenda Aralar, ksatria Teodosio de Goñi yang sedang menebus dosa diselamatkan Santo Mikael, yang datang bersama Allah dan memenggal naga itu; kisah ini terkait biara San Miguel de Aralar dan peralihan ke agama Kristen. Dalam dongeng lain, seorang pangeran membunuh Herensuge berkepala tujuh dengan bantuan raksasa bermata satu Tartalo.",
      "en": "In the Aralar legend, the penitent knight Teodosio de Goñi is saved by Saint Michael, who comes with God and beheads the dragon; the tale is tied to the monastery of San Miguel de Aralar and the turn to Christianity. In another tale a prince kills the seven-headed Herensuge with the help of the one-eyed giant Tartalo.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "herensuge-c09",
        "herensuge-c10",
        "herensuge-c11",
        "herensuge-c12"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "herensuge-c01",
      "source_id": "herensuge-s1",
      "quote": "Herensuge is the name for a mythical dragon in the Basque language. In Basque mythology, dragons appear sparingly, sometimes with seven heads. Herensuge often also appear in the form of a serpent.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Herensuge adalah nama naga mitis dalam bahasa Basque; dalam mitologi Basque naga jarang muncul, kadang berkepala tujuh, dan Herensuge sering juga berwujud ular.",
        "en": "Herensuge is the Basque name for a mythical dragon; dragons appear rarely in Basque mythology, sometimes with seven heads, and Herensuge often also takes the form of a serpent."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herensuge-c02",
      "source_id": "herensuge-s1",
      "quote": "The seven heads were believed to be the offspring of the Herensuge dragon. When the little dragons were fully grown, they would fall off their mother's head.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketujuh kepala itu dipercaya anak-anak Herensuge; setelah dewasa, naga-naga kecil itu lepas dari kepala induknya.",
        "en": "The seven heads were believed to be the Herensuge's offspring; when grown, the little dragons fell off their mother's head."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herensuge-c03",
      "source_id": "herensuge-s1",
      "quote": "Only the god Sugaar is associated with this creature but more often with a serpent.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hanya dewa Sugaar yang dikaitkan dengan makhluk ini, meski lebih sering dengan ular.",
        "en": "Only the god Sugaar is linked with this creature, though more often with a serpent."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herensuge-c04",
      "source_id": "herensuge-s2",
      "quote": "ist in der Folklore des Baskenlands ein teuflischer Höhlengeist, der in der Gestalt einer Schlange erscheint. Der Überlieferung zufolge hat er sieben Köpfe und kann fliegen (die Quellen variieren in dieser Aussage).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Jerman, ia roh gua jahat yang muncul berwujud ular, berkepala tujuh, dan dapat terbang, meski sumber berbeda soal ini.",
        "en": "According to the German article it is a devilish cave spirit appearing as a serpent, with seven heads and able to fly, though sources differ on this."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herensuge-c05",
      "source_id": "herensuge-s2",
      "quote": "Mit seinem Atem lockt er Menschen und Tiere in seine Höhle, um sie zu verschlingen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dengan napasnya ia menarik manusia dan hewan ke guanya untuk dilahap.",
        "en": "With its breath it draws people and animals into its cave to devour them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herensuge-c06",
      "source_id": "herensuge-s3",
      "quote": "Herensuge habite cavernes, montagnes et gouffres, et ses attaques terrifient les bergers et troupeaux ; il est au centre de nombreux récits héroïques.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Herensuge menghuni gua, gunung, dan jurang, serangannya menakutkan gembala dan ternak, dan ia menjadi pusat banyak kisah kepahlawanan.",
        "en": "Herensuge lives in caves, mountains and chasms, its attacks terrify shepherds and flocks, and it is at the heart of many heroic tales."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herensuge-c07",
      "source_id": "herensuge-s3",
      "quote": "Ses noms varient, mais la permanence du suffixe « suge » (couleuvre, serpent) suffit à justifier sa nature. C'est en effet un serpent.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya beragam, tetapi akhiran suge (\"ular\") yang selalu ada menunjukkan sifatnya sebagai ular.",
        "en": "Its names vary, but the constant suffix suge ('snake') shows its serpent nature."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herensuge-c08",
      "source_id": "herensuge-s3",
      "quote": "On trouve les noms de Erensugue, Erensuge, Iraunsuge, Lerensuge, Herainsuge, Errensuge, Hensuge, Edensugue, Edeinsuge, Edaansuge, Igensuge, Iraunsuge.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama-nama lainnya antara lain Erensugue, Iraunsuge, Lerensuge, Herainsuge, Errensuge, Edensugue, dan Igensuge.",
        "en": "Other names include Erensugue, Iraunsuge, Lerensuge, Herainsuge, Errensuge, Edensugue and Igensuge."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herensuge-c09",
      "source_id": "herensuge-s1",
      "quote": "A legend describes a Navarrese knight, Teodosio de Goñi, who while making penance for double parricide in the Aralar Range rescues a woman that had been given as ransom to the dragon.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebuah legenda menceritakan ksatria Navarra Teodosio de Goñi, yang saat menjalani penebusan dosa karena membunuh kedua orang tuanya di Pegunungan Aralar menyelamatkan seorang perempuan yang diserahkan sebagai tebusan kepada naga.",
        "en": "A legend tells of the Navarrese knight Teodosio de Goñi who, doing penance in the Aralar range for killing both parents, rescues a woman given to the dragon as ransom."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herensuge-c10",
      "source_id": "herensuge-s1",
      "quote": "the knight prays to Saint Michael to save him. In Heaven, the archangel is notified, but refuses to enter the fight without God. The archangel arrives with God over his head and decapitates the dragon, liberating Teodosio from his chains and ending his penance.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ksatria itu berdoa kepada Santo Mikael; sang malaikat agung menolak bertarung tanpa Allah, lalu datang dengan Allah di atas kepalanya, memenggal naga itu, dan membebaskan Teodosio dari rantai serta penebusannya.",
        "en": "The knight prays to Saint Michael; the archangel refuses to fight without God, then arrives with God above his head, beheads the dragon and frees Teodosio from his chains and his penance."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herensuge-c11",
      "source_id": "herensuge-s1",
      "quote": "This legend is associated to the monastery of San Miguel de Aralar. It has been interpreted as a way of justifying the break from the religion and customs of pagan Basques and the adoption of Christianity and, specifically, the veneration for St. Michael.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda ini terkait biara San Miguel de Aralar dan ditafsirkan sebagai pembenaran peralihan dari agama pagan Basque ke agama Kristen, khususnya pemujaan Santo Mikael.",
        "en": "The legend is tied to the monastery of San Miguel de Aralar and has been read as justifying the shift from pagan Basque religion to Christianity, especially the cult of St Michael."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herensuge-c12",
      "source_id": "herensuge-s1",
      "quote": "With the help of the Tartalo, the disguised prince, is able to kill the creature after three battles.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah Tartalo yang berterima kasih, seorang pangeran yang menyamar membunuh Herensuge berkepala tujuh setelah tiga kali bertarung dengan bantuan Tartalo.",
        "en": "In the tale of the Grateful Tartalo, a disguised prince kills the seven-headed Herensuge after three battles with the Tartalo's help."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Jentil adalah bangsa raksasa berbulu dalam mitologi Basque, pembangun dolmen dan pelempar batu antargunung, yang lenyap ke bumi saat cahaya kelahiran Kristus muncul.",
    "en": "The jentil are a race of hairy giants in Basque mythology, dolmen builders and mountain-to-mountain stone throwers, who vanished into the earth when the light of Christ's birth appeared.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "jentil-c01",
      "jentil-c02",

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Jentil atau jentilak, dari bahasa Latin gentilis, \"orang kafir\", adalah bangsa raksasa Basque yang dianggap pembangun dolmen dan monumen megalitik lain. Mereka berbulu, begitu tinggi hingga dapat berjalan di laut, dan melempar batu dari gunung ke gunung, yang menjelaskan bongkah batu terpencil dan bahkan permainan pilota; sebagian orang mengaitkan kekalahan Roland di Roncevaux dengan mereka. Mereka menemukan pengolahan logam dan gergaji serta mengajari manusia bertani.",
      "en": "The jentil or jentilak, from Latin gentilis, 'gentile', are a Basque race of giants credited with building dolmens and other megaliths. They were hairy, tall enough to wade in the sea, and threw rocks from mountain to mountain, explaining lone boulders and even the game of pilota; some tied Roland's defeat at Roncevaux to them. They invented metalworking and the saw and taught humans to farm.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "jentil-c01",
        "jentil-c02",
        "jentil-c03",
        "jentil-c04",
        "jentil-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ketika awan bercahaya pertanda kelahiran Kristus (Kixmi) muncul, mereka bersembunyi di bawah dolmen di lembah Arratzaren atau menjatuhkan diri dari gunung; hanya Olentzero yang tersisa dan kini muncul saat Natal. Sekitar selusin legenda \"Akhir Kaum Jentil\" dikenal sampai Bigorre dan Couserans, dan nama tempat seperti jentilarri dan jentilzulo mengenang mereka. Mereka tampaknya mewakili bangsa Basque pra-Kristen sendiri.",
      "en": "When a bright cloud heralding Christ's birth (Kixmi) appeared, they hid under a dolmen in the Arratzaren valley or leapt from a mountain; only Olentzero remained and now appears at Christmas. About a dozen 'End of the Gentiles' legends are known as far as Bigorre and Couserans, and place names such as jentilarri and jentilzulo recall them. They seem to stand for the pre-Christian Basques themselves.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "jentil-c01",
      "source_id": "jentil-s1",
      "quote": "The jentil (or jentilak with the Basque plural), are a race of giants in Basque mythology. This word meaning gentile, from Latin gentilis, was used to refer to pre-Christian civilizations and in particular to the builders of megalithic monuments, to which the other Basque mythical legend the Mairuak are involved too.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jentil (jamak Basque: jentilak) adalah bangsa raksasa dalam mitologi Basque; kata ini berarti \"orang kafir\", dari bahasa Latin gentilis, dan dipakai untuk peradaban pra-Kristen, khususnya pembangun monumen megalitik, yang juga dikaitkan dengan Mairu (mairuak).",
        "en": "The jentil (Basque plural jentilak) are a race of giants in Basque mythology; the word means 'gentile', from Latin gentilis, and was used for pre-Christian civilisations, especially the builders of megalithic monuments, with which the Mairu (mairuak) are also linked."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jentil-c02",
      "source_id": "jentil-s1",
      "quote": "The jentil were believed to have lived alongside the Basque people. They were hairy and so tall that they could walk in the sea and throw rocks from one mountain to another.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jentil dipercaya hidup berdampingan dengan orang Basque; mereka berbulu dan begitu tinggi sehingga dapat berjalan di laut dan melempar batu dari satu gunung ke gunung lain.",
        "en": "The jentil were believed to have lived alongside the Basques; they were hairy and so tall they could walk in the sea and throw rocks from one mountain to another."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jentil-c03",
      "source_id": "jentil-s1",
      "quote": "This stone throwing has led to several tales and explanations for ancient stone buildings and large isolated rocks. Even the Basque ball game, pilota, is ascribed to these stone-throwers.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lemparan batu ini menjelaskan bangunan batu kuno dan bongkah batu besar yang terpencil, bahkan permainan bola Basque pilota dikaitkan dengan mereka.",
        "en": "This stone-throwing explains ancient stone buildings and big isolated rocks, and even the Basque ball game pilota is ascribed to them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jentil-c04",
      "source_id": "jentil-s1",
      "quote": "Some attributed to the jentil the defeat of Roland in the Battle of Roncevaux, where the Basques defeated the Frankish army by throwing rocks on them.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian orang mengaitkan kekalahan Roland dalam Pertempuran Roncevaux, ketika orang Basque mengalahkan pasukan Frank dengan melempar batu, dengan para jentil.",
        "en": "Some credited the jentil with Roland's defeat at Roncevaux, where the Basques beat the Frankish army by hurling rocks."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jentil-c05",
      "source_id": "jentil-s1",
      "quote": "They also were said to have invented metallurgy and the saw and first grew wheat, teaching humans to farm.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka juga dikatakan menemukan pengolahan logam dan gergaji, pertama kali menanam gandum, dan mengajari manusia bertani.",
        "en": "They were also said to have invented metalworking and the saw, first grown wheat and taught humans to farm."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jentil-c06",
      "source_id": "jentil-s1",
      "quote": "They disappeared into the earth under a dolmen in the Arratzaren valley in Navarra when a portentous luminous cloud – perhaps a star – appeared, said to have heralded the birth of Christ (Kixmi) and the end of the jentil age. Other stories say jentil threw themselves from a mountain.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka lenyap ke dalam bumi di bawah sebuah dolmen di lembah Arratzaren, Navarra, ketika muncul awan bercahaya, mungkin bintang, pertanda kelahiran Kristus (Kixmi) dan akhir zaman jentil; kisah lain menyebut mereka menjatuhkan diri dari gunung.",
        "en": "They vanished into the earth under a dolmen in the Arratzaren valley in Navarre when a portentous bright cloud, perhaps a star, appeared, heralding the birth of Christ (Kixmi) and the end of the jentil age; other stories say they threw themselves off a mountain."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jentil-c07",
      "source_id": "jentil-s1",
      "quote": "Only Olentzero remained, a giant who appears at Christmas and is reproduced as straw dolls.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hanya Olentzero yang tersisa, raksasa yang muncul saat Natal dan dibuat sebagai boneka jerami.",
        "en": "Only Olentzero remained, a giant who appears at Christmas and is made as straw dolls."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jentil-c08",
      "source_id": "jentil-s1",
      "quote": "Dolmens are   jentilarri or jentiletxe, harrespil are jentilbaratz, caves can be jentilzulo or jentilkoba.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dolmen disebut jentilarri atau jentiletxe, lingkaran batu harrespil disebut jentilbaratz, dan gua disebut jentilzulo atau jentilkoba.",
        "en": "Dolmens are called jentilarri or jentiletxe, harrespil stone circles jentilbaratz, and caves jentilzulo or jentilkoba."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jentil-c09",
      "source_id": "jentil-s2",
      "quote": "Au Pays basque, mais aussi en Bigorre et en Couserans, on connait une douzaine de légendes avec le titre « La fin des Gentils » qui racontent les circonstances de la disparition d'une « race » qui précéda les humains actuels, à l'âge d'or des Pyrénées.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di negeri Basque, Bigorre, dan Couserans dikenal sekitar selusin legenda berjudul \"Akhir Kaum Jentil\" tentang lenyapnya bangsa yang mendahului manusia sekarang pada zaman keemasan Pyrenees.",
        "en": "In the Basque Country, Bigorre and Couserans about a dozen legends titled 'The End of the Gentiles' tell how a race that preceded present humans vanished in the Pyrenees' golden age."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jentil-c10",
      "source_id": "jentil-s3",
      "quote": "Los gentiles parecen representar al propio pueblo vasco precristiano, montañés por excelencia.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, para jentil tampaknya mewakili bangsa Basque pra-Kristen sendiri, penghuni gunung sejati.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, the jentilak seem to represent the pre-Christian Basque people themselves, mountain-dwellers par excellence."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia en, fr, dan es; belum ada sumber di luar Wikipedia yang dikutip untuk entri ini."
    },
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}

    "id": "Mairu adalah raksasa mitologi Basque yang membangun dolmen dan kromlek di pegunungan, namanya berarti \"orang Moor\" atau non-Kristen.",
    "en": "The Mairu are giants of Basque mythology who built the mountain dolmens and cromlechs, their name meaning 'Moor' or non-Christian.",

    {
      "id": "Mairu atau mairuak, juga disebut Maideak, Mairiak, atau Intxisu, adalah raksasa mitologi Basque yang membangun dolmen dan kromlek di pegunungan, seperti di Zugarramurdi, Oiartzun, dan Buluntza; kromlek kecil disebut Mairu-baratze, \"kebun Mairu\", dan di Oiartzun dianggap kuburan mereka. Namanya mungkin berarti \"orang Moor\" dalam arti non-Kristen, dan asal-usulnya dianggap setua mouros encantados Portugal.",
      "en": "The Mairu or mairuak, also called Maideak, Mairiak or Intxisu, are Basque giants who built mountain dolmens and cromlechs such as those of Zugarramurdi, Oiartzun and Buluntza; small cromlechs are called Mairu-baratze, 'Mairu's garden', and at Oiartzun are thought to be their graves. Their name may mean 'Moor' in the sense of non-Christian, and their origin is held to be as old as the Portuguese mouros encantados.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "mairu-c01",
      "source_id": "mairu-s1",
      "quote": "Mairu (plural: mairuak), also called Maideak, Mairiak, Saindi Maidi (in Lower Navarre), Intxisu in the Bidasoa valley are creatures of Basque mythology.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mairu (jamak: mairuak), juga disebut Maideak, Mairiak, Saindi Maidi (di Navarra Bawah), atau Intxisu di lembah Bidasoa, adalah makhluk mitologi Basque.",
        "en": "The Mairu (plural mairuak), also called Maideak, Mairiak, Saindi Maidi (in Lower Navarre) or Intxisu in the Bidasoa valley, are creatures of Basque mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mairu-c02",
      "source_id": "mairu-s1",
      "quote": "The Mairu were giants who built dolmens or harrespil. Like the dolmens, they are only found in mountains. They are often associated with lamia, though these are known in all the Basque Country.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mairu adalah raksasa pembangun dolmen atau lingkaran batu harrespil yang, seperti dolmen, hanya ada di pegunungan, dan sering dikaitkan dengan lamia.",
        "en": "The Mairu were giants who built dolmens or harrespil stone circles, found like the dolmens only in the mountains, and are often linked with the lamia."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mairu-c03",
      "source_id": "mairu-s1",
      "quote": "Mairu could mean \"moor\" in Basque. This term is used with the sense of 'non-Christian' to refer to former civilizations or megalithic monuments.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mairu mungkin berarti \"orang Moor\" dalam bahasa Basque, dipakai dalam arti \"non-Kristen\" untuk peradaban lama atau monumen megalitik.",
        "en": "Mairu may mean 'Moor' in Basque, used in the sense of 'non-Christian' for former civilisations or megalithic monuments."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mairu-c04",
      "source_id": "mairu-s1",
      "quote": "The origin of the Mairu is thought to be as old as the \"mouros encantados\" in Portuguese (Spanish: moros encantados), who are thought to be the remnant of old pre-Roman deities.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Asal-usul Mairu dianggap setua mouros encantados di Portugal (Spanyol: moros encantados), yang dianggap sisa dewa-dewa pra-Romawi.",
        "en": "The Mairu's origin is thought to be as old as the Portuguese mouros encantados (Spanish moros encantados), considered remnants of old pre-Roman deities."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mairu-c05",
      "source_id": "mairu-s2",
      "quote": "On attribue aux Mairuak la construction de certains dolmens et cromlechs comme ceux d'Ibañeta à Zugarramurdi, d'Oiartzun et de Buluntza.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mairuak dianggap membangun sejumlah dolmen dan kromlek, seperti di Ibañeta di Zugarramurdi, Oiartzun, dan Buluntza.",
        "en": "The Mairuak are credited with building dolmens and cromlechs such as those of Ibañeta at Zugarramurdi, Oiartzun and Buluntza."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mairu-c06",
      "source_id": "mairu-s2",
      "quote": "Mairu-baratze (littéralement 'jardin de Mairu') désigne un petit cromlech.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mairu-baratze, harfiah \"kebun Mairu\", adalah sebutan untuk kromlek kecil.",
        "en": "Mairu-baratze, literally 'Mairu's garden', means a small cromlech."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mairu-c07",
      "source_id": "mairu-s2",
      "quote": "Dans certaines légendes, Mairu-beso est le bras desséché ou un os du bras de Mairu qui possède des vertus mystérieuses. Plus concrètement, c'est le bras d'un nouveau-né non baptisé.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam sebagian legenda, Mairu-beso adalah lengan kering atau tulang lengan Mairu yang berkhasiat gaib, yang sebenarnya lengan bayi yang belum dibaptis.",
        "en": "In some legends Mairu-beso is the dried arm or arm bone of a Mairu with mysterious powers, in practice the arm of an unbaptised newborn."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mairu-c08",
      "source_id": "mairu-s2",
      "quote": "Dans les narrations, le protagoniste utilise cet os comme torche pour s'éclairer et parfois pour endormir les habitants de la maison dans laquelle il s'est introduit.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisahnya, tokoh utama memakai tulang itu sebagai obor dan kadang untuk menidurkan penghuni rumah yang dimasukinya.",
        "en": "In the stories the protagonist uses the bone as a torch and sometimes to put the household he has broken into to sleep."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mairu-c09",
      "source_id": "mairu-s2",
      "quote": "Mairu (Mairuak au pluriel en basque) est un géant ou jentil construisant des châteaux, dolmens et autres monuments mégalithiques dans la mythologie du Pays basque.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, Mairu adalah raksasa atau jentil yang membangun kastel, dolmen, dan monumen megalitik lain dalam mitologi Basque.",
        "en": "According to the French article, the Mairu is a giant or jentil who builds castles, dolmens and other megaliths in Basque mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mairu-c10",
      "source_id": "mairu-s3",
      "quote": "En Oyarzun llaman intxitxu a los constructores de los crómlechs de la región y dicen que son cementerios de estos genios.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, di Oiartzun para pembangun kromlek disebut intxitxu, dan kromlek itu dikatakan kuburan mereka.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, in Oiartzun the builders of the cromlechs are called intxitxu and the cromlechs are said to be their graveyards."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Trasgu adalah peri rumah usil dari Spanyol utara dan Portugal utara, lelaki kecil pincang bertopi merah dengan tangan berlubang yang membuat keributan malam atau membantu pekerjaan rumah.",
    "en": "The trasgu is a mischievous house goblin of northern Spain and northern Portugal, a limping red-capped little man with a holed hand who makes night mischief or does the housework.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "trasgu-c01",
      "trasgu-c02",
      "trasgu-c03",
      "trasgu-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Trasgu, trasgo, atau trasno adalah peri rumah dalam tradisi Galisia, Asturias, Cantabria, dan Portugal utara; namanya mungkin dari bahasa Latin transgredī, \"melanggar\". Ia lelaki kecil berkulit gelap yang pincang kaki kanan, berpakaian dan bertopi merah, dengan lubang di tangan kiri. Pada malam hari ia membuat keributan dan memindahkan barang, tetapi bila diperlakukan baik ia mengerjakan pekerjaan rumah.",
      "en": "The trasgu, trasgo or trasno is a house goblin of Galician, Asturian, Cantabrian and northern Portuguese tradition; its name may come from Latin transgredī, 'to transgress'. He is a small dark man limping on his right leg, dressed and capped in red, with a hole in his left hand. At night he makes noise and moves things, but if treated well he does the housework.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "trasgu-c01",
        "trasgu-c02",
        "trasgu-c03",
        "trasgu-c04",
        "trasgu-c09",
        "trasgu-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia sulit diusir: dalam sebuah kisah ia ikut pindah sambil membawa lampu yang tertinggal. Cara mengusirnya adalah memberi tugas mustahil, seperti memungut jawawut dengan tangannya yang berlubang. Ia punya banyak nama daerah seperti Cornín dan Xuan Dos Camíos, dan dari Trás-os-Montes mitosnya menyebar hingga melahirkan Saci di Brasil.",
      "en": "He is hard to shake off: in one tale he moves house with the family, carrying the lamp they forgot. The way to expel him is an impossible task, such as picking up millet with his holed hand. He has many local names such as Cornín and Xuan Dos Camíos, and from Trás-os-Montes the myth spread to give rise to the Saci in Brazil.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "trasgu-c05",
        "trasgu-c06",
        "trasgu-c07",
        "trasgu-c08"
      ]
    }

      "title": "Тресго",

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "trasgu-c01",
      "source_id": "trasgu-s1",
      "quote": "The trasgo, trasno or trasgu is a mythological creature present in the tradition of several cultures of what is now northern Spain, especially in  Galician, Asturian and Cantabrian traditional culture, it is also found in legends of North Portugal.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Trasgo, trasno, atau trasgu adalah makhluk mitologis dalam tradisi beberapa budaya Spanyol utara, terutama Galisia, Asturias, dan Cantabria, juga dalam legenda Portugal utara.",
        "en": "The trasgo, trasno or trasgu is a mythological creature in the traditions of several cultures of northern Spain, especially Galician, Asturian and Cantabrian, and also in legends of northern Portugal."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "trasgu-c02",
      "source_id": "trasgu-s1",
      "quote": "It is a domestic goblin with a mischievous and nervous character. It is often represented as a tiny man who limps with his right leg; he has dark skin, wears red clothes and a pointy red hat. He has a hole in his left hand.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia peri rumah yang usil dan gelisah, sering digambarkan sebagai lelaki kecil yang pincang kaki kanan, berkulit gelap, berpakaian merah dan bertopi merah runcing, dengan lubang di tangan kirinya.",
        "en": "It is a mischievous, restless house goblin, often shown as a tiny man limping on his right leg, dark-skinned, in red clothes and a pointed red hat, with a hole in his left hand."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "trasgu-c03",
      "source_id": "trasgu-s1",
      "quote": "Nocturnal noises are attributed to him, and also small pranks like changing the location of objects. He enters homes at night when the inhabitants are asleep.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Suara-suara malam dan keusilan kecil seperti memindahkan barang dianggap ulahnya; ia masuk ke rumah pada malam hari ketika penghuninya tidur.",
        "en": "Night noises and small pranks such as moving objects are blamed on him; he enters homes at night while the household sleeps."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "trasgu-c04",
      "source_id": "trasgu-s1",
      "quote": "On the other hand, when he is treated well, he does house chores during the night.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebaliknya, bila diperlakukan baik, ia mengerjakan pekerjaan rumah pada malam hari.",
        "en": "On the other hand, when treated well, he does the housework at night."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "trasgu-c05",
      "source_id": "trasgu-s1",
      "quote": "In a tale, the inhabitants of a house abandoned it because of the trasgu. On their way to the new house, the woman asks her husband: \"Have we left anything?\" The trasgu, following them, answers: \"You have left the lamp, but I'm carrying it.\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam sebuah kisah, penghuni rumah pindah karena trasgu; di jalan sang istri bertanya apakah ada yang tertinggal, dan trasgu yang mengikuti mereka menjawab, \"Kalian meninggalkan lampu, tapi aku membawanya.\"",
        "en": "In one tale a household moves away because of the trasgu; on the way the wife asks whether they left anything, and the trasgu, following them, answers: 'You left the lamp, but I'm carrying it.'"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "trasgu-c06",
      "source_id": "trasgu-s1",
      "quote": "In order to expel a trasgu it is necessary to request of him an impossible task, like bringing a basket of water from the sea, picking up millet from the floor (it falls through the hole in his hand), and whitening a black sheep.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Untuk mengusir trasgu ia harus diberi tugas mustahil, seperti membawa air laut dalam keranjang, memungut biji jawawut dari lantai (yang jatuh lewat lubang di tangannya), atau memutihkan domba hitam.",
        "en": "To get rid of a trasgu one must set him an impossible task, such as carrying sea water in a basket, picking millet off the floor (it falls through the hole in his hand) or whitening a black sheep."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "trasgu-c07",
      "source_id": "trasgu-s1",
      "quote": "In Asturias, the trasgu is known by different names depending on the location. He is known as Trasno, Cornín or Xuan Dos Camíos in western Asturias.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Asturias trasgu punya nama berbeda menurut tempat, seperti Trasno, Cornín, atau Xuan Dos Camíos di Asturias barat.",
        "en": "In Asturias the trasgu has different names by place, such as Trasno, Cornín or Xuan Dos Camíos in western Asturias."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "trasgu-c08",
      "source_id": "trasgu-s1",
      "quote": "Trasgu or trasno (in Galician and Portuguese) is very common in the folklore of Trás-os-Montes and it was taken by the Portuguese throughout their empire originating similar myths like the Saci in Brazil.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Trasgu atau trasno sangat umum dalam cerita rakyat Trás-os-Montes dan dibawa orang Portugis ke seluruh kekaisaran mereka, melahirkan mitos serupa seperti Saci di Brasil.",
        "en": "The trasgu or trasno is very common in the folklore of Trás-os-Montes and was carried by the Portuguese across their empire, giving rise to similar myths such as the Saci in Brazil."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "trasgu-c09",
      "source_id": "trasgu-s2",
      "quote": "Del latín transgredī ('el que transgrede, el que rompe con la ley')  se le denomina trasgo, trasno o tardo en Galicia",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, namanya dari bahasa Latin transgredī, \"yang melanggar\", dan di Galisia ia disebut trasgo, trasno, atau tardo.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, the name comes from Latin transgredī, 'one who transgresses', and in Galicia he is called trasgo, trasno or tardo."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "trasgu-c10",
      "source_id": "trasgu-s3",
      "quote": "Тресго наиболее известны в астурийской мифологии. Это домашний гоблин с озорным и нервным характером.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Rusia juga menyebut trasgu makhluk paling terkenal dalam mitologi Asturias, peri rumah yang usil dan gelisah.",
        "en": "The Russian article also calls the trasgu the best-known being of Asturian mythology, a mischievous, restless house goblin."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Aloja atau dona d'aigua adalah peri air mitologi Katalan berambut emas yang tinggal di danau dan mata air Pyrenees, hidup ribuan tahun, dan dapat menjadi burung air.",
    "en": "The aloja or dona d'aigua is a golden-haired water fairy of Catalan mythology living in Pyrenean lakes and springs, who lives for thousands of years and can turn into a water bird.",
    "claim_ids": [

      "aloja-mythology-c02",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Aloja, juga dona d'aigua atau goja, adalah perempuan air mitologi Katalan yang tinggal dekat air tawar yang dingin, dari danau Pyrenees sampai gua dan sungai bawah tanah. Mereka berambut emas atau merah, bermata hijau atau biru, suka bercermin di bawah bulan purnama, dan dapat menjadi burung air. Mereka tidak abadi tetapi hidup ribuan tahun, mendukung kelahiran dan kesuburan, dan dianggap adaptasi lokal nimfa.",
      "en": "The aloja, also dona d'aigua or goja, is a Catalan water-woman living by cold fresh water, from Pyrenean tarns to underground caves and streams. They have gold or red hair and green or blue eyes, like to see their reflection under the full moon, and can become water birds. Not immortal but living thousands of years, they favour births and fertility and are seen as a local form of the nymphs.",

    {
      "id": "Danau tempat mereka mandi mendidih bila orang asing masuk. Dalam kisah pernikahan, suami yang membocorkan rahasia istrinya ditinggalkan bersama hilangnya kekayaannya. Di Mallorca, Maria Enganxa tinggal di sumur dan menarik anak dengan kaitnya.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "aloja-mythology-c01",
      "source_id": "aloja-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "In Catalan mythology an aloja ... also known as dona d'aigua, goja or paitida, is a feminine being that lives in places with fresh water. These \"water-women\" are said to be able to turn into water blackbirds.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Katalan, aloja (juga dona d'aigua, goja, atau paitida) adalah makhluk perempuan yang tinggal di tempat berair tawar dan konon dapat menjelma menjadi burung air (dipper).",
        "en": "In Catalan mythology an aloja (also dona d'aigua, goja or paitida) is a female being living where there is fresh water, said to be able to turn into a water blackbird (dipper)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aloja-mythology-c02",
      "source_id": "aloja-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "According to legend, these women are not immortal, but can live for thousands of years and retain their youth.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka tidak abadi, tetapi dapat hidup ribuan tahun dan tetap muda.",
        "en": "They are not immortal but can live for thousands of years and stay young."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aloja-mythology-c03",
      "source_id": "aloja-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "It is said that the lakes where the dones d'aigua bathe can boil in anger if any stranger enters them. In Mallorca, the most famous water-woman is Maria Enganxa. According to tradition, she lives inside all the wells and cisterns and takes all the children that pass near them with her hook.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Danau tempat mereka mandi konon mendidih karena marah bila orang asing masuk; di Mallorca dona d'aigua paling terkenal adalah Maria Enganxa, yang tinggal di semua sumur dan bak air dan menarik anak yang lewat dengan kaitnya.",
        "en": "The lakes where they bathe are said to boil with anger if a stranger enters; in Mallorca the most famous is Maria Enganxa, who lives in every well and cistern and hooks children who pass by."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aloja-mythology-c04",
      "source_id": "aloja-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "According to myth, water-women are nocturnal; have shimmering gold or red hair, and emerald or deep blue eyes; wear fine, rich clothes; and enjoy viewing their reflections in lakes on full-moon nights.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka makhluk malam berambut emas atau merah berkilau, bermata hijau zamrud atau biru tua, berpakaian indah, dan suka bercermin di danau pada malam bulan purnama.",
        "en": "They are nocturnal, with shimmering gold or red hair and emerald or deep blue eyes, wear fine clothes and like to see their reflection in lakes on full-moon nights."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aloja-mythology-c05",
      "source_id": "aloja-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Often, one of the conditions is that the husband cannot reveal that his wife is a water-woman. If he does, the water-woman leaves him and disappears with his fortune.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah pernikahan dengan manusia, syaratnya sering bahwa sang suami tidak boleh membocorkan bahwa istrinya dona d'aigua; bila melanggar, ia pergi membawa kekayaannya.",
        "en": "In tales of marriage with humans the condition is often that the husband must not reveal his wife is a water-woman; if he does, she leaves and takes his fortune."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aloja-mythology-c06",
      "source_id": "aloja-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Son genios que favorecen los nacimientos y la fertilidad, dadoras de vida y regeneradoras constantes de la naturaleza. Son en general benévolas, aunque debe tenerse prudencia si uno se las encuentra.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, mereka roh yang mendukung kelahiran dan kesuburan, pemberi kehidupan dan pemulih alam, umumnya baik hati meski perlu berhati-hati bila bertemu mereka.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article they are spirits favouring births and fertility, life-givers and renewers of nature, generally kindly though one should be careful on meeting them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aloja-mythology-c07",
      "source_id": "aloja-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "La palabra «goja» (cat. pl. «goges») podría venir del catalán antiguo o tal vez del occitano, con el significado de 'doncella joven', 'muchacha', 'niña'",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata goja mungkin berasal dari bahasa Katalan kuno atau Oksitan, berarti \"gadis muda\".",
        "en": "The word goja may come from Old Catalan or Occitan, meaning 'young maiden'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aloja-mythology-c08",
      "source_id": "aloja-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Habitan siempre cerca de aguas gélidas y azules: los estanques del Pirineo, torrentes y saltos de agua, fuentes de los bosques, pozas, manantiales, grutas húmedas, y corrientes y lagos subterráneos.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka selalu tinggal dekat air biru yang dingin: danau Pyrenees, sungai deras dan air terjun, mata air hutan, kolam, gua lembap, dan sungai serta danau bawah tanah.",
        "en": "They always live near cold blue waters: Pyrenean tarns, torrents and waterfalls, forest springs, pools, damp caves and underground streams and lakes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aloja-mythology-c09",
      "source_id": "aloja-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Son una adaptación local de las ninfas como representaciones de las fuerzas (femeninas) de la naturaleza.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka adaptasi lokal dari nimfa sebagai perwujudan kekuatan feminin alam.",
        "en": "They are a local adaptation of the nymphs as embodiments of nature's feminine forces."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aloja-mythology-c10",
      "source_id": "aloja-mythology-s3",
      "quote": "Viven en os Pireneus, en zonas an bi ha augua, ya sigan fuents, ríos, torrents u espelungas humedas.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Aragon juga menyebut mereka tinggal di Pyrenees, di tempat berair seperti mata air, sungai, aliran deras, atau gua lembap.",
        "en": "The Aragonese article also says they live in the Pyrenees where there is water, such as springs, rivers, torrents or damp caves."
      }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Anguane adalah nimfa air Alpen Italia utara, perempuan cantik berkaki kambing atau belah yang tinggal di dekat air, menikahi manusia, dan membawa kemalangan bila dihina.",
    "en": "Anguane are Alpine water nymphs of northern Italian folklore, beautiful goat- or cloven-footed women living by water who marry humans and bring misfortune if insulted.",
    "claim_ids": [

      "anguane-c03",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Anguane, dalam bahasa Ladin Ganes, adalah nimfa air dari cerita rakyat Alpen Italia utara; namanya mungkin dari bahasa Latin aqua, \"air\". Mereka tinggal dekat danau, sungai, mata air, atau gua, mencuci linen, dan hanya terlihat saat fajar dan senja. Mereka bisa tampak sebagai perempuan muda cantik atau perempuan tua berkaki kambing, berpayudara panjang yang disampirkan ke punggung untuk menyusui anak dalam keranjang.",
      "en": "The anguane, Ganes in Ladin, are water nymphs of northern Italian Alpine folklore; the name may come from Latin aqua, 'water'. They live by lakes, brooks, springs or caves, wash linen, and appear only at dawn and dusk. They may look like beautiful young women or old goat-footed hags, with long breasts flung over the shoulder to nurse children carried in baskets.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "anguane-c01",
        "anguane-c02",
        "anguane-c03",
        "anguane-c04",
        "anguane-c06",
        "anguane-c07",
        "anguane-c09",
        "anguane-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Mereka merayu pengembara, membantu perempuan mandul hamil, dan kadang menikahi manusia dengan syarat rambut pirangnya tidak disentuh, sambil memberi hadiah yang tak pernah habis. Siapa pun yang menghina mereka akan ditimpa kemalangan seumur hidup.",
      "en": "They seduce travellers, help barren women conceive, and sometimes marry humans on condition their blond hair is not touched, giving gifts that never run out. Whoever insults them suffers lifelong misfortune.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "anguane-c03",
        "anguane-c05",
        "anguane-c08"
      ]

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "anguane-c01",
      "source_id": "anguane-s1",
      "quote": "Anguane are mythical creatures in Northern Italian folklore, which are traditionally depicted as cloven-footed water nymphs with beautiful faces and long breasts.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anguane adalah makhluk mitis cerita rakyat Italia utara yang biasanya digambarkan sebagai nimfa air berkaki belah dengan wajah cantik dan payudara panjang.",
        "en": "Anguane are mythical beings of northern Italian folklore, usually shown as cloven-footed water nymphs with beautiful faces and long breasts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anguane-c02",
      "source_id": "anguane-s1",
      "quote": "They were said to have carried their children in baskets held on their backs, and to have been able to throw their breasts over their backs to nurse these children.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka konon menggendong anak dalam keranjang di punggung dan dapat menyampirkan payudaranya ke belakang untuk menyusui anak itu.",
        "en": "They were said to carry their children in baskets on their backs and to fling their breasts over their shoulders to nurse them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anguane-c03",
      "source_id": "anguane-s1",
      "quote": "Anguane were believed to resided in or nearby lakes. According to myth, Anguane seduce male wayfarers and help infertile women become pregnant.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anguane dipercaya tinggal di dalam atau dekat danau; menurut mitos mereka merayu lelaki pengembara dan membantu perempuan mandul hamil.",
        "en": "Anguane were thought to live in or near lakes; by myth they seduce male travellers and help barren women conceive."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anguane-c04",
      "source_id": "anguane-s2",
      "quote": "Als Anguana (von lat. aqua = Wasser; auch Aguana, Guana, Gana, Eguana, Agana, Enguana) bezeichnet man eine Dämonin aus der ladinischen bzw. romanischsprachigen Mythologie",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Jerman, anguana (dari bahasa Latin aqua, \"air\"; juga Aguana, Guana, Gana, Eguana, Agana, Enguana) adalah makhluk halus perempuan dari mitologi Ladin dan Rumansch.",
        "en": "According to the German article, the anguana (from Latin aqua, 'water'; also Aguana, Guana, Gana, Eguana, Agana, Enguana) is a female spirit of Ladin and Romansh mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anguane-c05",
      "source_id": "anguane-s2",
      "quote": "Es heißt, dass sie ein Leben lang Unglück bringen sollen, wenn man sie beleidigt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Konon mereka membawa kemalangan seumur hidup bila dihina.",
        "en": "They are said to bring lifelong misfortune if insulted."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anguane-c06",
      "source_id": "anguane-s2",
      "quote": "Sie können sowohl als schöne, junge, attraktive Frauen, als auch als alte, hässliche Frauen mit Ziegenfüßen in Erscheinung treten.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka dapat muncul sebagai perempuan muda yang cantik atau sebagai perempuan tua buruk rupa berkaki kambing.",
        "en": "They can appear as beautiful young women or as ugly old women with goat's feet."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anguane-c07",
      "source_id": "anguane-s2",
      "quote": "Angeblich leben sie an Bächen, Quellen oder in Höhlen und waschen Leinen. Auch sollen sie sich nur zur Morgen- und Abenddämmerung blicken lassen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka konon tinggal di tepi sungai kecil, mata air, atau gua sambil mencuci kain linen, dan hanya terlihat saat fajar dan senja.",
        "en": "They supposedly live by brooks, springs or in caves washing linen, and show themselves only at dawn and dusk."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anguane-c08",
      "source_id": "anguane-s2",
      "quote": "Zuweilen sollen sie auch Ehen mit den Menschen eingehen, ähnlich wie auch die Feen, allerdings darf der Mann dann nicht ihr blondes Haar berühren. Darüber hinaus sollen sie „unendliche Geschenke“ (also Dinge, die nie zu Ende gehen) überreichen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadang mereka menikah dengan manusia seperti peri, tetapi sang suami tidak boleh menyentuh rambut pirangnya; mereka juga memberi \"hadiah tanpa akhir\", benda yang tak pernah habis.",
        "en": "They sometimes marry humans like fairies do, but the husband must not touch their blond hair; they also give 'endless gifts', things that never run out."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anguane-c09",
      "source_id": "anguane-s2",
      "quote": "In der ladinischen Sprache werden sie verkürzt als Gana (Einzahl) oder Ganes (Mehrzahl) bezeichnet.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam bahasa Ladin mereka disebut singkat Gana (tunggal) atau Ganes (jamak).",
        "en": "In Ladin they are called Gana (singular) or Ganes (plural) for short."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anguane-c10",
      "source_id": "anguane-s3",
      "quote": "L'anguana è una creatura associata all'acqua, dalle caratteristiche in parte simili a quelle di una ninfa e tipica della mitologia alpina.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Italia, anguana adalah makhluk air mirip nimfa yang khas mitologi Alpen.",
        "en": "According to the Italian article, the anguana is a water being somewhat like a nymph, typical of Alpine mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "anguane-c11",
      "source_id": "anguane-s3",
      "quote": "Storie sulle anguane (agane nelle tradizioni friulane, carniche e ladine dolomitiche) si ricordano soprattutto nelle regioni pedemontane e montane",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Italia, kisah anguane (agane dalam tradisi Friuli, Carnia, dan Ladin Dolomit) terutama diingat di daerah kaki gunung dan pegunungan.",
        "en": "According to the Italian article, tales of the anguane (agane in Friulian, Carnic and Dolomite Ladin traditions) are remembered mainly in the foothill and mountain regions."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Bicorn dan Chichevache adalah sepasang binatang satire abad pertengahan: Bicorn gemuk karena memakan suami yang setia, Chichevache kurus karena istri yang patuh langka.",
    "en": "Bicorn and Chichevache are a pair of medieval satirical beasts: Bicorn is fat from eating devoted husbands, Chichevache thin because obedient wives are scarce.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Bicorn dan Chichevache adalah binatang dongeng dalam satire Eropa Abad Pertengahan dan Renaisans. Bicorn, setengah macan tutul setengah sapi berwajah manusia dan bertanduk dua, gemuk karena memangsa suami yang baik dan setia; Chichevache, \"sapi kurus\", kelaparan karena hanya memakan istri yang patuh. Dalam cerita rakyat Prancis keduanya disebut Bigorne dan Chicheface, dan Bigorne di sana tampak seperti harimau.",
      "en": "Bicorn and Chichevache are fabulous beasts of medieval and Renaissance European satire. Bicorn, part panther, part cow with a human face and two horns, is fat from devouring kind, devoted husbands; Chichevache, the 'thin cow', starves because it eats only obedient wives. In French folklore they are Bigorne and Chicheface, and the Bigorne there looks like a tiger.",

        "bicorn-and-chichevache-c01",
        "bicorn-and-chichevache-c02",

        "bicorn-and-chichevache-c07",
        "bicorn-and-chichevache-c08",
        "bicorn-and-chichevache-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Chaucer menyebut Chichevache dalam Canterbury Tales, dan John Lydgate menulis puisi Bycorne and Chychevache yang menjadikan keduanya suami istri. Di Poitou-Charentes Bigorne juga momok yang memakan anak dekat sumur, dan bicorn muncul dalam Harry Potter and the Chamber of Secrets.",
      "en": "Chaucer names Chichevache in the Canterbury Tales, and John Lydgate's poem Bycorne and Chychevache makes them husband and wife. In Poitou-Charentes the Bigorne is also a bogey eating children near wells, and the bicorn appears in Harry Potter and the Chamber of Secrets.",
      "claim_ids": [

      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bicorn dan Chichevache adalah binatang dongeng dalam karya satire Eropa Abad Pertengahan dan Renaisans; Bicorn, setengah macan tutul setengah sapi berwajah manusia, memangsa suami yang baik dan setia sehingga gemuk karena mangsanya melimpah.",
        "en": "Bicorn and Chichevache are fabulous beasts of European satire in the Middle Ages and Renaissance; Bicorn, part panther, part cow with a human face, devours kind and devoted husbands and is fat because they are plentiful."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bicorn-and-chichevache-c02",
      "source_id": "bicorn-and-chichevache-s1",
      "quote": "Chichevache, on the other hand, devours obedient wives and (because of their scarcity) is thin and starving.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Chichevache sebaliknya memangsa istri yang patuh sehingga kurus kelaparan karena mangsanya langka.",

    {
      "id": "bicorn-and-chichevache-c03",
      "source_id": "bicorn-and-chichevache-s1",
      "quote": "Geoffrey Chaucer mentions Chichevache in the envoy of the Clerk's Tale in his Canterbury Tales, ironically warning wives against the patience and obedience shown by Griselda in the story",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Geoffrey Chaucer menyebut Chichevache dalam penutup The Clerk's Tale dalam Canterbury Tales, secara ironis memperingatkan para istri agar tidak sesabar dan sepatuh Griselda.",
        "en": "Geoffrey Chaucer names Chichevache in the envoy of the Clerk's Tale in the Canterbury Tales, ironically warning wives against Griselda's patience and obedience."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bicorn-and-chichevache-c04",
      "source_id": "bicorn-and-chichevache-s1",
      "quote": "Chaucer may have borrowed the French term chichifache (\"thin face\") and blended it with vache (\"cow\") to make the similar term chichevache (\"thin or meagre cow\").",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Chaucer mungkin meminjam istilah Prancis chichifache (\"wajah kurus\") dan memadukannya dengan vache (\"sapi\") menjadi chichevache (\"sapi kurus\").",
        "en": "Chaucer may have borrowed the French chichifache ('thin face') and blended it with vache ('cow') into chichevache ('thin cow')."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bicorn-and-chichevache-c05",
      "source_id": "bicorn-and-chichevache-s1",
      "quote": "In the early fifteenth century John Lydgate wrote \"Bycorne and Chychevache\", a 133-line poem in 7-line stanzas, probably from a French original.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada awal abad ke-15 John Lydgate menulis Bycorne and Chychevache, puisi 133 baris dalam bait tujuh baris, mungkin dari karya asli Prancis.",
        "en": "In the early 15th century John Lydgate wrote Bycorne and Chychevache, a 133-line poem in 7-line stanzas, probably from a French original."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bicorn-and-chichevache-c06",
      "source_id": "bicorn-and-chichevache-s1",
      "quote": "Lydgate describes the two beasts as husband and wife.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lydgate menggambarkan kedua binatang itu sebagai suami dan istri.",
        "en": "Lydgate presents the two beasts as husband and wife."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bicorn-and-chichevache-c07",
      "source_id": "bicorn-and-chichevache-s2",
      "quote": "Bigorne et Chicheface sont des animaux fantastiques du folklore en Anjou, en Normandie, mais aussi en Auvergne.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, Bigorne dan Chicheface adalah binatang khayalan dalam cerita rakyat Anjou, Normandia, dan Auvergne.",
        "en": "According to the French article, Bigorne and Chicheface are fantastic animals of the folklore of Anjou, Normandy and Auvergne."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bicorn-and-chichevache-c08",
      "source_id": "bicorn-and-chichevache-s2",
      "quote": "Bigorne au contraire, qui  avait l'apparence d'un  tigre, était repu, gros et gras, car il mangeait les hommes tyrannisés par leur femme",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bigorne, yang berwujud seperti harimau, kenyang dan gemuk karena memangsa lelaki yang ditindas istrinya.",
        "en": "Bigorne, which looked like a tiger, was full, fat and sleek because it ate men tyrannised by their wives."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bicorn-and-chichevache-c09",
      "source_id": "bicorn-and-chichevache-s2",
      "quote": "La Bigorne dans le Poitou-Charentes était un animal fantastique qui attirait et mangeait les petits enfants qui s'approchaient un peu trop des points d'eau comme les mares, les puits etc.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Poitou-Charentes, Bigorne adalah binatang khayalan yang memikat dan memakan anak kecil yang terlalu dekat dengan kolam atau sumur.",
        "en": "In Poitou-Charentes the Bigorne was a fantastic animal that lured and ate small children who came too close to ponds or wells."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bicorn-and-chichevache-c10",
      "source_id": "bicorn-and-chichevache-s3",
      "quote": "Двурог, помимо обладания двумя рогами, имеет тело наполовину коровы и наполовину пантеры, а также человеческое лицо.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Rusia menyebut Bicorn, selain bertanduk dua, bertubuh setengah sapi setengah macan tutul dan berwajah manusia.",
        "en": "The Russian article says the Bicorn, besides its two horns, has a body half cow, half panther and a human face."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bicorn-and-chichevache-c11",
      "source_id": "bicorn-and-chichevache-s1",
      "quote": "A bicorn is mentioned in Harry Potter and the Chamber of Secrets. Its powdered horn is used as an ingredient in Polyjuice Potion.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bicorn disebut dalam Harry Potter and the Chamber of Secrets, dan bubuk tanduknya menjadi bahan Ramuan Polijus.",
        "en": "A bicorn is mentioned in Harry Potter and the Chamber of Secrets, its powdered horn an ingredient of Polyjuice Potion."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {
      "topic": {
        "id": "Wujud Bicorn",
        "en": "Bicorn's appearance"
      },
      "positions": [
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Setengah macan tutul setengah sapi berwajah manusia.",
            "en": "Part panther, part cow with a human face."
          },
          "claim_ids": [

            "en": "Like a tiger."
          },

  ],

    "id": "Căpcăun adalah raksasa berkepala anjing dari cerita rakyat Rumania yang menculik anak dan putri raja untuk dimakan.",
    "en": "The Căpcăun is a dog-headed ogre of Romanian folklore that kidnaps children and princesses to eat them.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Căpcăun adalah raksasa pemakan manusia dalam cerita rakyat Rumania yang menculik anak dan gadis, terutama putri raja, dan melambangkan kejahatan seperti Zmeu dan Balaur. Namanya tampaknya berarti \"kepala anjing\": ia berkepala anjing, kadang bermata empat atau bermata di tengkuk, dan menurut artikel bahasa Rusia bermulut dua, satu untuk menelan anak bulat-bulat dan satu untuk memuntahkan sisanya.",
      "en": "The Căpcăun is a man-eating ogre of Romanian folklore who kidnaps children and girls, mostly princesses, and embodies evil like the Zmeu and Balaur. Its name seems to mean 'dog-head': it has a dog's head, sometimes four eyes or eyes in the nape, and in the Russian article two mouths, one to swallow children whole and one to spit out the rest.",
