  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "capcaun-c01",
      "source_id": "capcaun-s1",
      "quote": "A Căpcăun is a creature in Romanian folklore, depicted as an ogre who kidnaps children or young ladies (mostly princesses). It represents evil, as do its counterparts Zmeu and the Balaur.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Căpcăun adalah makhluk cerita rakyat Rumania, raksasa pemakan manusia yang menculik anak atau gadis muda, terutama putri raja, dan melambangkan kejahatan seperti Zmeu dan Balaur.",
        "en": "The Căpcăun is a creature of Romanian folklore, an ogre who kidnaps children or young ladies, mostly princesses, and embodies evil like the Zmeu and the Balaur."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "capcaun-c02",
      "source_id": "capcaun-s1",
      "quote": "The Romanian word appears to have meant \"Dog-head\" (căp being a form of cap, meaning \"head\", and căun a derivative of câine, \"dog\"). According to Romanian folkloric phantasy, the căpcăun has a dog head, sometimes with four eyes, with eyes in the nape, or with four legs, but whose main characteristic is anthropophagy.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata Rumania ini tampaknya berarti \"kepala anjing\" (căp dari cap, \"kepala\", dan căun dari câine, \"anjing\"); căpcăun berkepala anjing, kadang bermata empat, bermata di tengkuk, atau berkaki empat, dan ciri utamanya memakan manusia.",
        "en": "The Romanian word seems to mean 'dog-head' (căp from cap, 'head', and căun from câine, 'dog'); the căpcăun has a dog's head, sometimes four eyes, eyes in the nape or four legs, and its main trait is cannibalism."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "capcaun-c03",
      "source_id": "capcaun-s1",
      "quote": "In most Romanian publications of other European works the names of creatures such as Ogres or Trolls are usually translated as căpcăun.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam terjemahan Rumania karya Eropa lain, nama makhluk seperti ogre atau troll biasanya diterjemahkan sebagai căpcăun.",
        "en": "In Romanian translations of other European works, creatures such as ogres or trolls are usually rendered as căpcăun."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "capcaun-c04",
      "source_id": "capcaun-s1",
      "quote": "The term căpcăun also means \"Tatar chieftain\" or \"Turk chieftain\", as well \"pagan\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Istilah căpcăun juga berarti \"kepala suku Tatar\" atau \"kepala suku Turk\", serta \"kafir\".",
        "en": "The term căpcăun also means 'Tatar chieftain' or 'Turkish chieftain', and 'pagan'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "capcaun-c05",
      "source_id": "capcaun-s1",
      "quote": "Some linguists consider căpcăun to be an echo of a title or administrative rank, such as kapkan (also kavhan, kaphan, kapgan) used by various Central Asian tribes who invaded Eastern Europe during late antiquity and the medieval era, such as the Pannonian Avars, Bulgars and Pechenegs.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian ahli bahasa menganggap căpcăun gema gelar kapkan (juga kavhan, kaphan, kapgan) yang dipakai suku-suku Asia Tengah seperti Avar Pannonia, Bulgar, dan Pecheneg yang menyerbu Eropa Timur.",
        "en": "Some linguists see căpcăun as an echo of the title kapkan (also kavhan, kaphan, kapgan) used by Central Asian peoples such as the Pannonian Avars, Bulgars and Pechenegs who invaded Eastern Europe."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "capcaun-c06",
      "source_id": "capcaun-s2",
      "quote": "Это людоеды, заглатывающие одним из своих ртов детей целиком или большие куски человеческого мяса прямо с костями и всем остальным, а из другого — выплёвывающие несъедобные отходы. Рассказывали, что они ловили мальчиков и откармливали их на убой.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Rusia, mereka pemakan manusia yang menelan anak bulat-bulat dengan satu mulut dan memuntahkan sisa yang tak termakan dari mulut lain, serta menangkap anak laki-laki untuk digemukkan sebelum disembelih.",
        "en": "According to the Russian article, they are man-eaters that swallow children whole with one mouth and spit out the inedible remains from the other, and catch boys to fatten for slaughter."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "capcaun-c07",
      "source_id": "capcaun-s2",
      "quote": "Поверье об этих существах возможно происходит из греческого романа «Александрия», который был переведён на румынский язык в 1620 году",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kepercayaan ini mungkin berasal dari roman Yunani Alexandria, yang diterjemahkan ke bahasa Rumania pada 1620.",
        "en": "The belief may come from the Greek romance Alexandria, translated into Romanian in 1620."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "capcaun-c08",
      "source_id": "capcaun-s2",
      "quote": "В современной Румынии кэпкэуном называют злого, жестокого и бессердечного человека.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Rumania modern, căpcăun dipakai untuk menyebut orang yang jahat, kejam, dan tak berperasaan.",
        "en": "In modern Romania căpcăun is used for a wicked, cruel, heartless person."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "capcaun-c09",
      "source_id": "capcaun-s3",
      "quote": "Un căpcăun és una criatura del folklore romanès representada com un ogre que segresta nens o senyoretes (principalment princeses).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Katalan juga menyebut căpcăun makhluk cerita rakyat Rumania berupa raksasa pemakan manusia yang menculik anak atau gadis, terutama putri raja.",
        "en": "The Catalan article also calls the căpcăun a creature of Romanian folklore shown as an ogre who kidnaps children or young ladies, mostly princesses."
      }
    }
  ],

    "en": "Colapesce is a Messina youth turned merman in a legend of Sicily, said still to hold up a column supporting the island of Sicily on the seabed.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "cola-pesce-c01",
      "cola-pesce-c06",
      "cola-pesce-c09"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Colapesce atau Cola Pesce, \"Nikolas Ikan\", adalah legenda manusia ikan Italia yang tercatat sejak abad ke-12, dari Raimon Jordan sampai Walter Map, yang mengisahkan Nicholas Pipe yang hidup di bawah laut dan memperingatkan kapal akan badai. Dalam versi Palermo yang disunting Italo Calvino, ibunya mengutuknya menjadi setengah ikan karena terus berenang; versi Jerman menyebutnya putra nelayan dari Messina.",
      "en": "Colapesce or Cola Pesce, 'Nicholas Fish', is an Italian merman legend recorded from the 12th century, from Raimon Jordan to Walter Map, who tells of Nicholas Pipe living under the sea and warning ships of storms. In the Palermo version edited by Italo Calvino his mother curses him into half a fish for swimming all the time; the German version makes him a fisherman's son from Messina.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cola-pesce-c01",
        "cola-pesce-c02",
        "cola-pesce-c03",
        "cola-pesce-c04",
        "cola-pesce-c06",
        "cola-pesce-c11"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kaisar Friedrich II mengujinya dengan melempar cangkir, mahkota, lalu cincin ke laut; pada kali ketiga Colapesce tidak kembali. Ia melihat Sisilia atau Messina bertumpu pada tiga tiang yang salah satunya rusak, dan konon tinggal di dasar laut menopangnya; gempa terjadi saat ia memindahkan tiang ke bahu lain.",
      "en": "Emperor Frederick II tested him by throwing a cup, a crown and then a ring into the sea; the third time Colapesce did not return. He saw that Sicily or Messina rests on three columns, one damaged, and is said to stay on the seabed holding it up; earthquakes happen when he shifts it to the other shoulder.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "cola-pesce-c01",
      "source_id": "cola-pesce-s1",
      "quote": "Cola Pesce, also known as Pesce Cola (i.e., \"Nicholas Fish\") is an Italian folktale about a merman, mentioned in literature as early as the 12th century. Many variants and retellings have been recorded.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cola Pesce atau Pesce Cola (\"Nikolas Ikan\") adalah dongeng Italia tentang manusia ikan yang disebut dalam sastra sejak abad ke-12, dengan banyak varian.",
        "en": "Cola Pesce or Pesce Cola ('Nicholas Fish') is an Italian folktale of a merman mentioned in literature from the 12th century, with many variants."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cola-pesce-c02",
      "source_id": "cola-pesce-s1",
      "quote": "The first known literary mention was by a 12th-century poet, Raimon Jordon of Provencal, who was referred to a \"Nichola de Bar\" (Nicholas of Bari) who lived with the fishes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebutan sastra pertama berasal dari penyair abad ke-12 Raimon Jordan dari Provence tentang \"Nichola de Bar\" (Nikolas dari Bari) yang hidup bersama ikan.",
        "en": "The first literary mention is by the 12th-century Provençal poet Raimon Jordan, of a 'Nichola de Bar' (Nicholas of Bari) who lived among the fishes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cola-pesce-c03",
      "source_id": "cola-pesce-s1",
      "quote": "Walter Map recorded a story of \"Nicholas Pipe,\" who appeared like a normal human but had the ability to live under the sea for long periods of time, and would warn ships of storms.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Walter Map mencatat kisah Nicholas Pipe yang tampak seperti manusia biasa tetapi dapat lama hidup di bawah laut dan memperingatkan kapal tentang badai.",
        "en": "Walter Map recorded the story of Nicholas Pipe, who looked like a normal man but could live under the sea for long periods and warned ships of storms."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cola-pesce-c04",
      "source_id": "cola-pesce-s1",
      "quote": "In a version from Palermo, edited by Italo Calvino, Nick Fish spends all of his time swimming, until his frustrated mother curses him to turn into a fish. He instantly becomes half fish, with duck feet and a frog's throat.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam versi Palermo yang disunting Italo Calvino, Cola terus berenang sampai ibunya yang kesal mengutuknya menjadi ikan, dan seketika ia menjadi setengah ikan berkaki bebek dan bertenggorokan katak.",
        "en": "In a Palermo version edited by Italo Calvino, Cola swims all the time until his exasperated mother curses him into a fish, and he at once becomes half fish, with duck feet and a frog's throat."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cola-pesce-c05",
      "source_id": "cola-pesce-s1",
      "quote": "Nick Fish discovers that Messina rests on three columns, some of which are broken",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia mendapati Messina bertumpu pada tiga tiang, sebagian di antaranya patah.",
        "en": "He finds that Messina rests on three columns, some of them broken."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cola-pesce-c06",
      "source_id": "cola-pesce-s2",
      "quote": "Colapesce oder auch Cola Pesce, eigentlich Nikolaus, kurz Cola genannt, war Sohn eines Fischers aus Messina.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Jerman, Colapesce, sebenarnya Nikolaus atau Cola, adalah putra nelayan dari Messina.",
        "en": "According to the German article, Colapesce, really Nikolaus or Cola, was a fisherman's son from Messina."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cola-pesce-c07",
      "source_id": "cola-pesce-s2",
      "quote": "Der Kaiser ging daher mit einigen Beratern in einem Boot aufs Meer und warf eine Tasse ins Wasser, die sofort von Colapesce wieder heraufgeholt wurde.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kaisar Friedrich II ingin mengujinya, lalu melempar cangkir ke laut dan Colapesce segera mengambilnya kembali.",
        "en": "Emperor Frederick II, wanting to test him, threw a cup into the sea, and Colapesce brought it straight back up."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cola-pesce-c08",
      "source_id": "cola-pesce-s2",
      "quote": "Beim dritten Mal soll Friedrich einen Ring in noch tieferes Wasser geworfen haben, doch Colaspesce erschien nicht mehr zurück an der Oberfläche.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada kali ketiga Friedrich melempar cincin ke air yang lebih dalam, dan Colapesce tidak muncul lagi ke permukaan.",
        "en": "The third time Frederick threw a ring into deeper water, and Colapesce never came back to the surface."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cola-pesce-c09",
      "source_id": "cola-pesce-s2",
      "quote": "so sah Colapesce bei seinem letzten Tauchgang für Friedrich, dass Sizilien auf drei Säulen gebaut ist, von denen eine verrostet war, und er beschloss, unter Wasser zu bleiben und für den Kaiser die Säule zu unterstützen, damit die Insel nicht unterginge und er stütze sie noch heute.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada penyelaman terakhirnya ia melihat Sisilia berdiri di atas tiga tiang, satu di antaranya berkarat, dan ia memutuskan tinggal di bawah air menopang tiang itu agar pulau itu tidak tenggelam, dan ia masih menopangnya sampai kini.",
        "en": "On his last dive he saw Sicily stands on three columns, one of them rusted, and decided to stay under water holding it up so the island would not sink, and he holds it still."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cola-pesce-c10",
      "source_id": "cola-pesce-s2",
      "quote": "Wenn die Insel bebt, so heißt es, sei er erschöpft und wechsle die Schulter.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Konon bila pulau itu berguncang, ia sedang lelah dan memindahkan tiang ke bahu lainnya.",
        "en": "When the island shakes, it is said, he is tired and switching shoulders."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cola-pesce-c11",
      "source_id": "cola-pesce-s3",
      "quote": "Cola pesce, o Colapesce, in catalano Peix Nicolau, è un personaggio immaginario del folclore europeo, la cui leggenda è stata tramandata oralmente in molte varianti e le cui origini risalgono al XII secolo.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Italia menyebut Colapesce, dalam bahasa Katalan Peix Nicolau, tokoh khayalan cerita rakyat Eropa yang legendanya diwariskan lisan dalam banyak varian sejak abad ke-12.",
        "en": "The Italian article calls Colapesce, Peix Nicolau in Catalan, an imaginary figure of European folklore whose legend has been passed down orally in many variants since the 12th century."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Iratxo adalah peri usil malam mitologi Basque yang membantu pekerjaan rumah bila dihormati, tetapi mengerjai orang yang menyinggungnya.",
    "en": "The iratxo is a nocturnal imp of Basque mythology that helps with housework when respected but plays tricks on those who offend it.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "iratxoak-c01",
      "iratxoak-c02",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Iratxoak adalah peri usil mitologi Basque, juga disebut irelu atau ireltxu, padanan goblin atau peri. Mereka makhluk malam yang tinggal di hutan, gua, dan reruntuhan dan kadang terkait harta tersembunyi. Bila diperlakukan baik mereka membantu pekerjaan rumah dan ladang; galtxagorriak, \"celana merah\", adalah jenisnya yang paling ramah. Bila tersinggung mereka mengerjai orang.",
      "en": "The iratxoak are the imps of Basque mythology, also called irelu or ireltxu, the local goblins or fairies. They are nocturnal, living in forests, caves and ruins and sometimes linked with hidden treasure. Treated well they help with house and farm work; the galtxagorriak, 'red pants', are the friendliest kind. If offended they play tricks.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "iratxoak-c01",
        "iratxoak-c02",
        "iratxoak-c03",
        "iratxoak-c05",
        "iratxoak-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Artikel bahasa Prancis justru menyebut mereka pengiring iblis yang jahat. Pepatah tahun 1596 menyebut Iratxo mengambil keuntungan yang diperoleh secara curang. Namanya dikaitkan dengan pakis (iratze) atau dengan irasco, \"kambing kebiri\". Pada malam Santo Yohanes orang berkostum iratxo membakar harapan dan pikiran buruk dalam api unggun.",
      "en": "The French article instead calls them wicked companions of the devil. A 1596 proverb says Iratxo takes away ill-gotten gains. The name is tied to the fern (iratze) or to irasco, 'castrated goat'. On Saint John's Eve people dressed as iratxoak burn wishes and bad thoughts in the bonfire.",

    {
      "id": "iratxoak-c01",
      "source_id": "iratxoak-s1",
      "quote": "Iratxoak (sing.: iratxo) are the imps of Basque mythology. Other names for them include irelsuzko/irelzuzko, irelu, irelixu/ireltxu, and iretxo.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Iratxoak (tunggal: iratxo) adalah peri usil mitologi Basque, juga disebut irelsuzko, irelu, ireltxu, atau iretxo.",
        "en": "Iratxoak (singular iratxo) are the imps of Basque mythology, also called irelsuzko, irelu, ireltxu or iretxo."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iratxoak-c02",
      "source_id": "iratxoak-s1",
      "quote": "They are generally considered the Basque equivalent of goblins or fairies, although a French-Basque dictionary from 1926 translates the word iratxo as spectre (ghost). They are said to assist in household and farming tasks if treated well. If offended, iratxoak may cause the offender minor troubles or target them with tricks.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka umumnya dianggap padanan Basque untuk goblin atau peri, meski kamus Prancis-Basque tahun 1926 menerjemahkan iratxo sebagai hantu; bila diperlakukan baik mereka membantu pekerjaan rumah dan ladang, tetapi bila tersinggung mereka membuat masalah kecil dan mengerjai orang.",
        "en": "They are generally seen as the Basque goblins or fairies, though a 1926 French-Basque dictionary renders iratxo as 'spectre'; treated well they help with house and farm work, but if offended they cause small troubles and play tricks."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iratxoak-c03",
      "source_id": "iratxoak-s1",
      "quote": "They are said to be found in many places, including forests, caves, and ruins. In some legends, iratxoak are associated with hidden treasure. Iratxoak are considered to be nocturnal.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka ada di banyak tempat seperti hutan, gua, dan reruntuhan, dalam sebagian legenda dikaitkan dengan harta tersembunyi, dan dianggap makhluk malam.",
        "en": "They are found in many places such as forests, caves and ruins, are tied to hidden treasure in some legends, and are nocturnal."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iratxoak-c04",
      "source_id": "iratxoak-s1",
      "quote": "During Saint John's Eve festivities, people dressed as iratxoak may collect wishes and unwanted thoughts from attendees and toss them into a bonfire.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam perayaan malam Santo Yohanes, orang yang berkostum iratxo mengumpulkan harapan dan pikiran buruk para pengunjung lalu melemparkannya ke api unggun.",
        "en": "At Saint John's Eve festivities, people dressed as iratxoak collect wishes and unwanted thoughts from the crowd and throw them into a bonfire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iratxoak-c05",
      "source_id": "iratxoak-s1",
      "quote": "Galtxagorriak are a house spirit variety of iratxo that are more helpful to humans. Their name translates to \"red-pants\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Galtxagorriak adalah jenis iratxo roh rumah yang lebih suka membantu manusia; namanya berarti \"celana merah\".",
        "en": "Galtxagorriak are a house-spirit kind of iratxo, more helpful to humans; their name means 'red pants'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iratxoak-c06",
      "source_id": "iratxoak-s1",
      "quote": "Some scholars believe that the word iratxo may be related to the word irasco, meaning \"castrated goat\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian sarjana menduga kata iratxo berkaitan dengan irasco, \"kambing kebiri\".",
        "en": "Some scholars think iratxo may be related to irasco, 'castrated goat'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iratxoak-c07",
      "source_id": "iratxoak-s2",
      "quote": "Iratxo, Gorritxiki, Aiharra-Haio sont des lutins maléfiques dans la mythologie basque. Ce sont des accompagnateurs du diable (deabru) et sont parfois décrits comme des diablotins.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, Iratxo, Gorritxiki, dan Aiharra-Haio adalah peri usil jahat pengiring iblis (deabru) yang kadang digambarkan sebagai iblis kecil.",
        "en": "According to the French article, Iratxo, Gorritxiki and Aiharra-Haio are malicious sprites, companions of the devil (deabru), sometimes described as imps."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iratxoak-c08",
      "source_id": "iratxoak-s2",
      "quote": "Son nom est cependant directement associé à la fougère (iratze en basque).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya dikaitkan langsung dengan tumbuhan pakis (iratze dalam bahasa Basque).",
        "en": "Its name is directly linked to the fern (iratze in Basque)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iratxoak-c09",
      "source_id": "iratxoak-s2",
      "quote": "Un proverbe publié au XVIe siècle (Énonciations et Sentences de 1596) se lit comme suit : Gassoto yrabacia / yrachoen da yracia. Ce dicton nous dit que les bénéfices obtenus de manière abusive, Iratxo vous les enlève.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pepatah dalam kumpulan tahun 1596, \"Gassoto yrabacia / yrachoen da yracia\", berarti keuntungan yang diperoleh secara curang akan diambil Iratxo.",
        "en": "A proverb printed in 1596, 'Gassoto yrabacia / yrachoen da yracia', says that ill-gotten gains are taken away by Iratxo."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iratxoak-c10",
      "source_id": "iratxoak-s3",
      "quote": "Iratxo (en plural en euskera: Iratxoak) es un tipo de duende de la mitología vasca de antiguas leyendas vizcaínas.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Spanyol menyebut iratxo sejenis peri rumah mitologi Basque dari legenda lama Biscaya.",
        "en": "The Spanish article calls the iratxo a kind of sprite of Basque mythology from old Biscayan legends."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {
      "topic": {
        "id": "Sifat iratxo",
        "en": "Nature of the iratxo"
      },
      "positions": [
        {
          "summary": {

    {

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Lariosauro adalah monster Danau Como, Italia, yang lahir dari laporan surat kabar rekaan tahun 1946 dan penampakan-penampakan berikutnya.",
    "en": "Lariosauro is the Lake Como monster of Italy, born of a hoaxed newspaper report in 1946 and later sightings.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "lariosauro-c01",
      "lariosauro-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Lariosauro adalah monster yang konon tinggal di Danau Como. Pada November 1946 Corriere comasco memberitakan dua pemburu melihat monster berkepala jambul sepanjang 2–3 meter bersisik cokelat kemerahan, tetapi berita itu rekaan pers; La Provincia kemudian menyebutnya mungkin ikan sturgeon. Julukannya diambil dari Lariosaurus balsami, reptil prasejarah yang fosilnya ditemukan di danau itu.",
      "en": "Lariosauro is a monster said to live in Lake Como. In November 1946 the Corriere comasco reported two hunters seeing a crested monster 2–3 metres long with reddish-brown scales, but the report was a press hoax; La Provincia later suggested a sturgeon. Its nickname comes from Lariosaurus balsami, a prehistoric reptile whose fossils were found at the lake.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lariosauro-c01",
        "lariosauro-c02",
        "lariosauro-c03",

      "en": "Other sightings were reported in 1954, 1957 and 2003, including a 10–12 m eel at Lecco, but Giorgio Castiglioni explained them as an otter, a hoax, a pike and a shoal of fish.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "lariosauro-c01",
      "source_id": "lariosauro-s1",
      "quote": "In Italian folklore, Lariosauro is a lake monster said to live in Lake Como in Italy, about 50 km (30 miles) north of Milan.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Italia, Lariosauro adalah monster danau yang konon tinggal di Danau Como, sekitar 50 km di utara Milan.",
        "en": "In Italian folklore Lariosauro is a lake monster said to live in Lake Como, about 50 km (30 miles) north of Milan."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lariosauro-c02",
      "source_id": "lariosauro-s1",
      "quote": "A sighting by two hunters was reported that on 18 November 1946 at Pian di Spagna (a reserve separated from Lake Como), was reported in the local newspaper Corriere comasco, but it was completely a hoax perpetrated by the press.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penampakan oleh dua pemburu pada 18 November 1946 di Pian di Spagna dilaporkan surat kabar Corriere comasco, tetapi sepenuhnya rekaan pers.",
        "en": "A sighting by two hunters on 18 November 1946 at Pian di Spagna was reported in the Corriere comasco, but it was entirely a press hoax."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lariosauro-c03",
      "source_id": "lariosauro-s1",
      "quote": "Achille Combi (who wrote the piece in  Corriere comasco) wrote that the crested head of the monster emerged out of water, its body length was 2–3 meters, and \"bristling with extremely hard, reddish-brown scales\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Achille Combi, penulis berita itu, menyebut kepala monster yang berjambul muncul dari air, panjang tubuhnya 2–3 meter, dan dipenuhi sisik keras berwarna cokelat kemerahan.",
        "en": "Achille Combi, who wrote the piece, said the monster's crested head rose from the water, its body was 2–3 metres long and 'bristling with extremely hard, reddish-brown scales'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lariosauro-c04",
      "source_id": "lariosauro-s1",
      "quote": "La Provincia published a follow-up piece a few days later suggesting it might have been a sturgeon.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Beberapa hari kemudian La Provincia menulis bahwa itu mungkin ikan sturgeon.",
        "en": "A few days later La Provincia suggested it might have been a sturgeon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lariosauro-c05",
      "source_id": "lariosauro-s1",
      "quote": "The creature earned the nickname lariosauro, but this was the same name legitimately used a century before to name a prehistoric reptile whose fossilized remains were found by the lake (Lariosaurus balsami).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Makhluk itu dijuluki lariosauro, nama yang seabad sebelumnya dipakai untuk reptil prasejarah yang fosilnya ditemukan di tepi danau (Lariosaurus balsami).",
        "en": "The creature was nicknamed lariosauro, the name used a century earlier for a prehistoric reptile whose fossils were found by the lake (Lariosaurus balsami)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lariosauro-c06",
      "source_id": "lariosauro-s1",
      "quote": "Skeptic researcher Giorgio Castiglioni, who studied these cases, thinks that the animal of 1954 was an otter, the monster of August 1957 a hoax, the beast of September 1957 possibly a pike, and the 2003 eel was a group of fish swimming together.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peneliti skeptis Giorgio Castiglioni menilai penampakan 1954 adalah berang-berang, monster Agustus 1957 rekaan, binatang September 1957 mungkin ikan pike, dan belut 2003 sekelompok ikan yang berenang bersama.",
        "en": "The sceptical researcher Giorgio Castiglioni judged the 1954 animal an otter, the August 1957 monster a hoax, the September 1957 beast possibly a pike and the 2003 eel a group of fish swimming together."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lariosauro-c07",
      "source_id": "lariosauro-s2",
      "quote": "En 2003 se avistó una anguila gigante de unos 10 – 12 m, en Lecco.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, pada 2003 terlihat belut raksasa sepanjang 10–12 m di Lecco.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, in 2003 a giant eel some 10–12 m long was sighted at Lecco."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lariosauro-c08",
      "source_id": "lariosauro-s3",
      "quote": "1946 m. liudininkai pranešė, kad matė į roplį panašų gyvūną, plaukusį ežere.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Lituania menyebut pada 1946 saksi melaporkan melihat hewan mirip reptil berenang di danau.",
        "en": "The Lithuanian article says that in 1946 witnesses reported seeing a reptile-like animal swimming in the lake."
      }
    }
  ],

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "La Encantada adalah perempuan tersihir dalam banyak legenda Spanyol yang muncul pada malam Santo Yohanes sambil menyisir rambut panjangnya dengan sisir emas, menunggu dibebaskan.",
    "en": "La Encantada is the enchanted woman of many Spanish legends who appears on St John's Eve combing her long hair with a golden comb, waiting to be freed.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "legend-of-la-encantada-c01",
      "legend-of-la-encantada-c02",
      "legend-of-la-encantada-c09"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "La Encantada, \"yang tersihir\", adalah nama umum untuk legenda di banyak tempat di Spanyol tentang perempuan muda berambut panjang yang muncul pada malam Santo Yohanes sambil menyisir rambut dengan sisir emas, dengan cermin dan janji pernikahan. Di Las Camarillas ia bertanya kepada yang lewat apakah mereka memilih sisir atau dirinya, dan gembala yang memilih sisir membuatnya tetap tersihir; di Rojales ia meminta digendong ke Sungai Segura untuk mematahkan kutukannya.",
      "en": "La Encantada, 'the enchanted one', is the generic name for legends in many Spanish places about a long-haired young woman who appears on St John's Eve combing her hair with a golden comb, with a mirror and a promise of marriage. At Las Camarillas she asked passers-by whether they preferred the comb or her, and a shepherd who chose the comb left her enchanted; at Rojales she asks to be carried to the Segura River to break her curse.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "legend-of-la-encantada-c01",
        "legend-of-la-encantada-c02",
        "legend-of-la-encantada-c08",
        "legend-of-la-encantada-c09",
        "legend-of-la-encantada-c10",
        "legend-of-la-encantada-c11",
        "legend-of-la-encantada-c12"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia dianggap berkerabat dengan Lamia, Moura, Mari, Mairu, Anjana, Xana, dan bahkan Xtabay Meksiko. Nama encantada umum dalam toponimi Spanyol dan sering menandai situs arkeologi; bentuk mora encantada tidak berarti situs itu dari masa Muslim.",
      "en": "She is thought akin to the Lamias, Mouras, Mari, Mairu, Anjanas, Xanas and even the Mexican Xtabay. The name encantada is common in Spanish place names and often marks archaeological sites; the form mora encantada does not mean the site is from the Muslim period.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "legend-of-la-encantada-c03",
        "legend-of-la-encantada-c04",
        "legend-of-la-encantada-c05",
        "legend-of-la-encantada-c06",
        "legend-of-la-encantada-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "legend-of-la-encantada-c01",
      "source_id": "legend-of-la-encantada-s1",
      "quote": "The Spanish legend of la Encantada is a generic name that refers to a set of oral traditions and legends mythological narrated in numerous Spanish localities.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda Spanyol la Encantada adalah nama umum untuk sekumpulan tradisi lisan dan legenda mitologis yang diceritakan di banyak tempat di Spanyol.",
        "en": "The Spanish legend of la Encantada is a generic name for a set of oral traditions and mythological legends told in many Spanish localities."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "legend-of-la-encantada-c02",
      "source_id": "legend-of-la-encantada-s1",
      "quote": "Although there are multiple local variants, a series of elements are common: the protagonist (a young woman with long hair), the time (St. John's Eve), the manifestation (combing her hair) and other elements (mirror, wedding, comb—generally gold).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Meski banyak varian, unsurnya sama: tokoh utama perempuan muda berambut panjang, waktu malam Santo Yohanes, penampakan sambil menyisir rambut, serta cermin, pernikahan, dan sisir yang biasanya emas.",
        "en": "Despite many local variants, common elements are a young long-haired woman, St John's Eve, her appearing while combing her hair, and a mirror, a wedding and a comb, usually golden."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "legend-of-la-encantada-c03",
      "source_id": "legend-of-la-encantada-s1",
      "quote": "La Encantada is supposedly closely related to mythological beings such as the Lamias, Mouras (Galician mythology), Mari and Mairu (Basque mythology), the Anjanas (Cantabrian mythology) and the Xanas (Asturian mythology)",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "La Encantada konon berkerabat dekat dengan Lamia, Moura (Galisia), Mari dan Mairu (Basque), Anjana (Cantabria), dan Xana (Asturias).",
        "en": "La Encantada is said to be closely related to the Lamias, the Mouras (Galicia), Mari and Mairu (Basque), the Anjanas (Cantabria) and the Xanas (Asturias)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "legend-of-la-encantada-c04",
      "source_id": "legend-of-la-encantada-s1",
      "quote": "Likewise, its relationship with the Mexican figure Xtabay suggests a very ancient and almost universal presence of the myth",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hubungannya dengan tokoh Meksiko Xtabay menunjukkan mitos ini sangat tua dan hampir universal.",
        "en": "Its link with the Mexican figure Xtabay suggests a very ancient, almost universal myth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "legend-of-la-encantada-c05",
      "source_id": "legend-of-la-encantada-s1",
      "quote": "The term encantada (\"enchanted\") is quite common in Spanish toponymy and microtoponymy and, normally, designates the apparition.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Istilah encantada, \"yang tersihir\", cukup umum dalam nama tempat di Spanyol dan biasanya menandai tempat penampakan.",
        "en": "The term encantada, 'enchanted', is common in Spanish place names and usually marks the apparition."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "legend-of-la-encantada-c06",
      "source_id": "legend-of-la-encantada-s1",
      "quote": "Gálmez de Fuentes considers said toponym as an explanation or a posteriori adaptation of the Pre-Roman term *kanto (\"stone, stony shore\").",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gálmez de Fuentes menganggap nama tempat itu adaptasi belakangan dari istilah pra-Romawi *kanto, \"batu, pantai berbatu\".",
        "en": "Gálmez de Fuentes sees the place name as a later adaptation of the pre-Roman *kanto, 'stone, stony shore'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "legend-of-la-encantada-c07",
      "source_id": "legend-of-la-encantada-s1",
      "quote": "Many times the toponym is presented under the form mora encantada (\"enchanted mora\"), which may lead to believe that the sites are from the period of the Muslim domination. This is not true, since many of them are earlier",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama tempat sering berbentuk mora encantada, yang bisa membuat orang mengira situs itu dari masa kekuasaan Muslim, padahal banyak yang lebih tua.",
        "en": "The place name often appears as mora encantada, suggesting sites from the Muslim period, though many are older."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "legend-of-la-encantada-c08",
      "source_id": "legend-of-la-encantada-s1",
      "quote": "In the early morning of St. John's Day (June 24), a very white lady with very long blonde hair used to appear at the foot of the Camareta cave",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Las Camarillas (Hellín), pada dini hari Santo Yohanes (24 Juni) seorang perempuan sangat putih berambut pirang sangat panjang biasa muncul di kaki gua Camareta.",
        "en": "At Las Camarillas (Hellín), in the early morning of St John's Day (24 June), a very white lady with very long blonde hair used to appear at the foot of the Camareta cave."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "legend-of-la-encantada-c09",
      "source_id": "legend-of-la-encantada-s1",
      "quote": "They say that on a certain occasion a shepherd passed by and when she asked him the question, he answered that it was the comb, and she exclaimed: \"Damn you, because of you I will continue to be enchanted!\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia bertanya kepada yang lewat mana yang lebih disukai, sisir atau dirinya; ketika seorang gembala memilih sisir, ia berseru, \"Terkutuklah kau, karenamu aku tetap tersihir!\"",
        "en": "She asked passers-by whether they liked the comb or her more; when a shepherd chose the comb she cried, 'Damn you, because of you I will stay enchanted!'"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "legend-of-la-encantada-c10",
      "source_id": "legend-of-la-encantada-s1",
      "quote": "If a brave man meets her, la Encantá will ask him to carry her in his arms to the Segura River to bathe her feet and thus break the curse.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Rojales, la Encantá meminta pemberani menggendongnya ke Sungai Segura untuk membasuh kakinya agar kutukannya patah.",
        "en": "At Rojales, la Encantá asks a brave man to carry her to the Segura River to bathe her feet and so break the curse."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "legend-of-la-encantada-c11",
      "source_id": "legend-of-la-encantada-s2",
      "quote": "La Leyenda española de la Encantada es un nombre genérico que hace referencia a un conjunto de tradiciones orales y leyendas mitológicas narradas en numerosas localidades españolas.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Spanyol juga menyebut la Encantada nama umum untuk tradisi lisan dan legenda di banyak tempat di Spanyol.",
        "en": "The Spanish article likewise calls la Encantada a generic name for oral traditions and legends in many Spanish places."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "legend-of-la-encantada-c12",
      "source_id": "legend-of-la-encantada-s3",
      "quote": "A Lenda espanhola da Encantada é um nome genérico que faz referência a um conjunto de tradições orais e lendas mitológicas narradas em numerosas localidades espanholas.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Portugis menyebut hal yang sama.",
        "en": "The Portuguese article says the same."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "lou-carcolh-c01",
      "source_id": "lou-carcolh-s1",
      "quote": "Lou Carcolh, or the Carcolh, is a mythical beast from French folklore. It is described as a large, slimy, snail-like serpent with hairy tentacles and a large shell. It is said to live in a cavern beneath Hastingues, a town in the Les Landes region in southwestern France.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lou Carcolh adalah binatang mitis cerita rakyat Prancis berupa ular mirip siput yang besar dan berlendir, bertentakel berbulu dan bercangkang besar, yang konon tinggal di gua di bawah Hastingues, Landes, Prancis barat daya.",
        "en": "Lou Carcolh is a mythical beast of French folklore, a large, slimy, snail-like serpent with hairy tentacles and a big shell, said to live in a cave beneath Hastingues in the Landes, south-west France."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lou-carcolh-c02",
      "source_id": "lou-carcolh-s1",
      "quote": "It is described to have a slime that could sometimes be seen long before the creature itself arrived, and its tentacles had a great reach that would be used to grab and drag prey into its cave without leaving.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lendirnya kadang terlihat jauh sebelum makhluk itu tiba, dan tentakelnya yang sangat panjang dipakai untuk meraih dan menyeret mangsa ke guanya tanpa ia keluar.",
        "en": "Its slime could sometimes be seen long before it arrived, and its long-reaching tentacles grabbed prey and dragged it into its cave without its leaving."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lou-carcolh-c03",
      "source_id": "lou-carcolh-s2",
      "quote": "Le carcolh (lou carcolh en gascon) est un escargot monstrueux appartenant au folklore du département français des Landes et propre à la ville de Hastingues. Toutefois, les références à cette créature ne sont pas antérieures au début du XXe siècle.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, carcolh adalah siput raksasa dari cerita rakyat Landes khas kota Hastingues, tetapi rujukan tentangnya tidak lebih tua dari awal abad ke-20.",
        "en": "According to the French article the carcolh is a monstrous snail of Landes folklore peculiar to Hastingues, but references to it are no older than the early 20th century."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lou-carcolh-c04",
      "source_id": "lou-carcolh-s2",
      "quote": "Mais, selon la légende, le tuc, qui offre protection, renferme également une caverne qui abrite un Carcolh, escargot formidable et monstrueux, qui serait la cause du départ des habitants, poussés par la peur, et du dépeuplement de la cité.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, bukit yang melindungi kota itu juga menyimpan gua tempat tinggal carcolh, siput mengerikan yang membuat penduduk ketakutan, pergi, dan kota pun sepi.",
        "en": "By legend the hill that sheltered the town also held a cave housing a carcolh, a fearsome monstrous snail that frightened the inhabitants away and emptied the town."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lou-carcolh-c05",
      "source_id": "lou-carcolh-s2",
      "quote": "Cette légende est favorisée par la forme arrondie du promontoire, rappelant celle d'une coquille d'escargot.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda ini didukung bentuk bukit yang bulat seperti cangkang siput.",
        "en": "The legend is encouraged by the hill's rounded shape, like a snail's shell."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lou-carcolh-c06",
      "source_id": "lou-carcolh-s2",
      "quote": "Concernant le Carcolh, la Revue de Gascogne de 1903 évoque un ... Escargot monstrueux qui a son gîte dans une vaste et sombre caverne au-dessus de laquelle est bâtie, d'après les traditions, la ville de Hastingues.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Revue de Gascogne tahun 1903 menyebut siput raksasa yang tinggal di gua besar dan gelap yang di atasnya, menurut tradisi, berdiri kota Hastingues.",
        "en": "The Revue de Gascogne of 1903 mentions a monstrous snail living in a vast, dark cave over which, by tradition, the town of Hastingues is built."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lou-carcolh-c07",
      "source_id": "lou-carcolh-s3",
      "quote": "Также считается, что солнечный свет пугает улитку, поэтому она не показывается на поверхности.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

        "en": "The Russian article says sunlight frightens the snail, so it never comes to the surface."
      }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Luisón adalah manusia serigala mitologi Guarani, putra ketujuh Tau dan Kerana, dan menurut kepercayaan Paraguay serta Argentina anak laki-laki ketujuh dalam keluarga akan menjadi Luisón.",
    "en": "The Luisón is the werewolf of Guarani mythology, seventh son of Tau and Kerana, and by belief in Paraguay and Argentina a family's seventh son will become one.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "luison-c01",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Luisón atau Lobizón adalah satu dari tujuh binatang legendaris Paraguay, putra ketujuh Tau dan Kerana yang menanggung kutukan terberat. Ia anjing raksasa yang mengerikan dan berada di persimpangan kehidupan dan kematian; versi Portugis menggambarkannya mirip serigala atau monyet bermata merah. Anak laki-laki ketujuh dipercaya berubah menjadi Luisón pada ulang tahun ke-13 atau pada malam bulan purnama hari Jumat dan Selasa.",
      "en": "Counted among Paraguay's seven legendary beasts, the Luisón or Lobizón is the seventh son of Tau and Kerana and bears the heaviest curse. He is a hideous giant dog standing at the crossroads of life and death; the Portuguese version likens him to a wolf or red-eyed monkey. A seventh son is believed to turn into a Luisón on his 13th birthday or on full-moon Friday and Tuesday nights.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "luison-c01",

      "en": "The myth may have merged with the werewolf lore settlers brought, through the maned wolf. Because some people once sacrificed their seventh sons, since the 19th century the President of Argentina has stood godparent to seventh sons, a custom made law by Juan Domingo Perón in 1973.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "luison-c01",
      "source_id": "luison-s1",
      "quote": "The Luison (Paraguayan Guaraní: Luisõ; Spanish: Luisón or Lobizón) is one of the seven legendary beasts of Paraguay, and is the seventh and last son of Tau and Kerana. The myth is known also in Argentina and says that in a family with seven male children, the last child will become Luisón.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Luisón (Guarani Paraguay: Luisõ; Spanyol: Luisón atau Lobizón) adalah satu dari tujuh binatang legendaris Paraguay, putra ketujuh dan terakhir Tau dan Kerana; mitos ini juga dikenal di Argentina dan menyebut bahwa dalam keluarga dengan tujuh anak laki-laki, anak terakhir akan menjadi Luisón.",
        "en": "The Luisón (Paraguayan Guarani Luisõ; Spanish Luisón or Lobizón) is one of the seven legendary beasts of Paraguay, the seventh and last son of Tau and Kerana; the myth is also known in Argentina and holds that in a family of seven sons the last will become a Luisón."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "luison-c02",
      "source_id": "luison-s1",
      "quote": "In the original version of the myth, Luisón was the seventh and last child of Tau and Kerana, and thus was the most accursed. He appeared to be a giant dog, and was said to be extremely ugly, even horrendous looking.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam versi asli, sebagai anak ketujuh Tau dan Kerana ia paling terkutuk, tampak seperti anjing raksasa yang sangat buruk rupa.",
        "en": "In the original myth, as the seventh child of Tau and Kerana he was the most accursed, looking like a giant, hideous dog."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "luison-c03",
      "source_id": "luison-s1",
      "quote": "The myth tells that the seventh son in a family will transform on his 13th birthday.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mitos menyebut anak laki-laki ketujuh dalam keluarga akan berubah pada ulang tahunnya yang ke-13.",
        "en": "The myth says a family's seventh son will transform on his 13th birthday."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "luison-c04",
      "source_id": "luison-s1",
      "quote": "The maned wolf, a slender, wolf-like, stilted canid that moves with shambling steps, fitted the popular depiction of the werewolf and ultimately it was syncretized with local guarani myths.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Serigala surai, anjing liar ramping berkaki panjang yang berjalan terseok, cocok dengan gambaran manusia serigala yang dibawa pendatang dan akhirnya berpadu dengan mitos Guarani setempat.",
        "en": "The maned wolf, a slender long-legged wolf-like canid with a shambling gait, fitted the werewolf image brought by settlers and was eventually blended with local Guarani myths."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "luison-c05",
      "source_id": "luison-s1",
      "quote": "It has been customary since the 19th century for the President of Argentina to be named the \"godparent\" of each seventh male child of a married couple. This custom arose because superstitious people sometimes sacrificed their seventh son out of fear of the \"possibility\" that a Luisón had been born.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejak abad ke-19 Presiden Argentina biasa menjadi wali baptis setiap anak laki-laki ketujuh, kebiasaan yang muncul karena orang yang percaya takhayul kadang mengorbankan anak ketujuh mereka karena takut ia Luisón.",
        "en": "Since the 19th century the President of Argentina has customarily been godparent to every seventh son, a custom born because superstitious people sometimes sacrificed their seventh son for fear he was a Luisón."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "luison-c06",
      "source_id": "luison-s1",
      "quote": "On 12 March 1973, President Juan Domingo Perón gave legal form to this custom through Decree No. 848, known as the presidential sponsorship law.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 12 Maret 1973 Presiden Juan Domingo Perón meresmikan kebiasaan ini lewat Dekret No. 848, yang dikenal sebagai undang-undang perwalian presiden.",
        "en": "On 12 March 1973 President Juan Domingo Perón made the custom law through Decree No. 848, the presidential sponsorship law."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "luison-c07",
      "source_id": "luison-s2",
      "quote": "Luisón es el séptimo hijo de Tau y Keraná en la mitología guaraní, sobre él cayó la mayor maldición que pesaba sobre los progenitores. Su solo nombre aterroriza. Este ser espeluznante se encuentra ubicado en la encrucijada de los caminos de la vida y de la muerte.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, sebagai putra ketujuh Tau dan Keraná ia menanggung kutukan terberat orang tuanya; namanya saja menakutkan, dan ia berada di persimpangan jalan kehidupan dan kematian.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, as the seventh son of Tau and Keraná he bears his parents' heaviest curse; his very name terrifies, and he stands at the crossroads of life and death."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "luison-c08",
      "source_id": "luison-s3",
      "quote": "Acredita-se que seja semelhante a um lobo sul-americano ou a um macaco de olhos vermelhos, com barbatanas de peixe e um enorme falo (de anta).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Portugis, ia dipercaya mirip serigala Amerika Selatan atau monyet bermata merah, bersirip ikan, dan berzakar besar seperti tapir.",
        "en": "According to the Portuguese article it is believed to look like a South American wolf or a red-eyed monkey, with fish fins and a huge tapir-like phallus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "luison-c09",
      "source_id": "luison-s3",
      "quote": "nas noites de lua cheia de sexta-feira e/ou terça-feira, o indivíduo se transformava em uma criatura com metade das característica",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada malam bulan purnama hari Jumat atau Selasa, orang itu berubah menjadi makhluk setengah binatang.",
        "en": "On full-moon nights falling on Friday and/or Tuesday the person turned into a half-animal creature."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Matagot adalah roh kucing hitam cerita rakyat Prancis selatan, sering diperoleh penyihir dengan menukar jiwa, yang membawa emas bagi tuannya bila dirawat baik.",
    "en": "The matagot is a black-cat spirit of southern French folklore, often obtained by a sorcerer in exchange for his soul, that brings gold to a master who treats it well.",

      "matagot-c04",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Matagot, juga mandragot atau chat d'argent, adalah roh berwujud kucing hitam dalam cerita rakyat Bretagne, Gascogne, Provence, dan Languedoc, yang menurut artikel bahasa Prancis diperoleh penyihir dengan menukar jiwanya. Ia umumnya jahat, tetapi matagot pembawa kekayaan bisa dipancing dengan ayam gemuk dan dibawa pulang tanpa menoleh; bila diberi suapan pertama setiap makan, ia pulang saat fajar membawa koin emas.",
      "en": "The matagot, also mandragot or chat d'argent, is a black-cat spirit of Breton, Gascon, Provençal and Languedoc folklore, which the French article says a sorcerer gets in exchange for his soul. It is generally evil, but a wealth-bringing matagot can be lured with a plump chicken and carried home without looking back; given the first mouthful at every meal, it comes home at dawn with gold coins.",

        "matagot-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia dapat melayani sembilan tuan dan konon membawa tuan terakhir ke neraka; di Gascogne pemilik yang sekarat lama menderita sampai membebaskannya, dan matagot yang diabaikan membalas dengan kejam. Matagot muncul dalam film Fantastic Beasts: The Crimes of Grindelwald.",
      "en": "It can serve nine masters and is said to lead the last to hell; in Gascony a dying owner suffers until he frees it, and a neglected matagot takes cruel revenge. The matagot appears in the film Fantastic Beasts: The Crimes of Grindelwald.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "matagot-c01",
      "source_id": "matagot-s1",
      "quote": "A Matagot is a spirit in French folklore that tends to take the form of a black cat.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Matagot adalah roh dalam cerita rakyat Prancis yang biasanya berwujud kucing hitam.",
        "en": "A matagot is a spirit of French folklore that usually takes the form of a black cat."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "matagot-c02",
      "source_id": "matagot-s1",
      "quote": "Matagots are generally evil, but some may prove helpful, like the \"magician cat\" said to bring wealth into a home if it is well fed.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Matagot umumnya jahat, tetapi sebagian dapat membantu, seperti \"kucing penyihir\" yang membawa kekayaan ke rumah bila diberi makan dengan baik.",
        "en": "Matagots are generally evil, but some help, like the 'magician cat' that brings wealth to a home if well fed."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "matagot-c03",
      "source_id": "matagot-s1",
      "quote": "Traditionally, a wealth-bringing matagot must be lured with a fresh, plump chicken, then carried home by its new owner without the human looking back.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Matagot pembawa kekayaan harus dipancing dengan ayam segar yang gemuk, lalu dibawa pulang tanpa pemiliknya menoleh ke belakang.",
        "en": "A wealth-bringing matagot must be lured with a fresh plump chicken and carried home without its new owner looking back."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "matagot-c04",
      "source_id": "matagot-s1",
      "quote": "If the cat is given the first mouthful of food and drink at every meal and otherwise treated well, it will bring the human good fortune. A matagot is said to be able to serve up to nine masters at once.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bila kucing itu diberi suapan pertama makanan dan minuman setiap kali makan dan dirawat baik, ia membawa keberuntungan; matagot konon dapat melayani sampai sembilan tuan sekaligus.",
        "en": "If the cat gets the first mouthful of food and drink at every meal and is well treated, it brings good luck; a matagot can serve up to nine masters at once."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "matagot-c05",
      "source_id": "matagot-s1",
      "quote": "In Gascony traditions, you must not keep the matagot all your life: if the owner is dying, they will suffer a long agony, as long as they don't free the matagot.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut tradisi Gascogne, matagot tidak boleh dipelihara seumur hidup: pemilik yang sekarat akan lama menderita selama belum membebaskannya.",
        "en": "In Gascon tradition one must not keep the matagot for life: a dying owner suffers a long agony until he frees it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "matagot-c06",
      "source_id": "matagot-s1",
      "quote": "In Fantastic Beasts: The Crimes of Grindelwald the matagot is a hairless, black, cat-like creature with the ability to multiply at will.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Fantastic Beasts: The Crimes of Grindelwald, matagot adalah makhluk mirip kucing hitam tak berbulu yang dapat menggandakan diri.",
        "en": "In Fantastic Beasts: The Crimes of Grindelwald the matagot is a hairless black cat-like creature that can multiply at will."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "matagot-c07",
      "source_id": "matagot-s2",
      "quote": "Le chat d'argent, mandragot ou matagot est, dans le folklore de Bretagne, de Gascogne, de Provence, et du Languedoc, un chat généralement noir et diabolique obtenu par un sorcier en échange de son âme.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, chat d'argent, mandragot, atau matagot dalam cerita rakyat Bretagne, Gascogne, Provence, dan Languedoc adalah kucing setan, biasanya hitam, yang diperoleh penyihir dengan menukar jiwanya.",
        "en": "According to the French article, the chat d'argent, mandragot or matagot of Breton, Gascon, Provençal and Languedoc folklore is a usually black, diabolical cat a sorcerer obtains in exchange for his soul."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "matagot-c08",
      "source_id": "matagot-s2",
      "quote": "Il est censé se promener dans quelques lieux mystérieux pendant la nuit, et revenir à l'aube avec un stock de louis d'or pour son maître. Si celui-ci le néglige ou ne le récompense pas, le chat s'offense et peut se venger cruellement.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berkeliaran di tempat misterius pada malam hari dan pulang saat fajar membawa koin emas louis untuk tuannya; bila diabaikan atau tidak diberi imbalan, ia tersinggung dan dapat membalas dengan kejam.",
        "en": "It roams mysterious places at night and returns at dawn with gold louis for its master; if neglected or unrewarded it takes offence and may take cruel revenge."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "matagot-c09",
      "source_id": "matagot-s2",
      "quote": "Parfois, ce chat serait censé servir non pas un, mais neuf maîtres, et conduire le dernier en enfer.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadang kucing ini melayani sembilan tuan dan membawa tuan terakhir ke neraka.",
        "en": "Sometimes the cat serves nine masters and leads the last to hell."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "matagot-c10",
      "source_id": "matagot-s3",
      "quote": "El matagot (en occità: lo matagòt), anomenat també mantagot (mantagòt), és un geni propi de la mitologia d'Occitània, conegut sobretot a la Provença i el Llenguadoc.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Katalan menyebut matagot, juga mantagot, roh mitologi Occitania yang terutama dikenal di Provence dan Languedoc.",
        "en": "The Catalan article calls the matagot, also mantagot, a spirit of Occitan mythology known especially in Provence and Languedoc."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia en, fr, dan ca; belum ada sumber di luar Wikipedia yang dikutip untuk entri ini."
    },
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Minairó adalah makhluk mungil pekerja keras dari Pyrenees Katalonia yang disimpan ribuan di dalam tabung jarum dan selalu menuntut pekerjaan, membawa kemakmuran bagi pemiliknya.",
    "en": "The minairó is a tiny hard-working being of the Catalan Pyrenees, kept by the thousand in a needle case and always demanding work, which brings its owner prosperity.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "minairo-c01",
      "minairo-c04",
      "minairo-c06"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Minairó, juga diablorí di Katalonia atau zaingorriak di negeri Basque, adalah makhluk sangat kecil yang di desa-desa Pyrenees disimpan ribuan dalam tabung jarum. Mereka berasal dari St John's-wort yang tumbuh di gua dalam yang dijaga raksasa dan naga dan hanya dapat diambil tengah malam Santo Yohanes. Mereka mendengung seperti lebah atau nyamuk dan begitu keluar menuntut, \"Apa yang kita kerjakan? Apa yang kita katakan?\"",
      "en": "The minairó, called diablorí by some in Catalonia and zaingorriak in the Basque lands, is a tiny being kept by the thousand in a needle case in Pyrenean villages. They come from St John's-wort, which grows in deep caves guarded by giants and dragons and can only be taken at midnight on St John's Day. They buzz like bees or mosquitoes and on coming out demand, 'What shall we do? What shall we say?'",
      "claim_ids": [
        "minairo-c01",
        "minairo-c02",
        "minairo-c03",
        "minairo-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pemilik tabung menjadi makmur, tetapi bila pembukanya tidak segera memberi tugas, mereka bisa membunuhnya; banyak hamparan batu longsoran di Pyrenees konon hasil kerja mereka ketika tabung terbuka tak sengaja. Di negeri Basque, roh kecil serupa disebut mamarroak atau galtzagorriak dan melindungi keluarga.",
      "en": "Owners of the case prospered, but if the opener did not give them a task at once they might kill him; many Pyrenean screes are said to be their work when a case was opened by accident. In the Basque Country similar small spirits are the mamarroak or galtzagorriak, who protect the family.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "minairo-c05",

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "minairo-c01",
      "source_id": "minairo-s1",
      "quote": "A minairó (Catalan pronunciation: [minəjˈɾo], plural minairons), also called manairó, menairó, diablorí in Catalonia or zaingorriak in the Basque country, is a tiny mythological being that some people save enclosed within a needle tube in some villages of the Pyrenees.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Minairó (jamak minairons), juga manairó, menairó, diablorí di Katalonia atau zaingorriak di negeri Basque, adalah makhluk mitis mungil yang di beberapa desa Pyrenees disimpan dalam tabung jarum.",
        "en": "A minairó (plural minairons), also manairó, menairó, diablorí in Catalonia or zaingorriak in the Basque Country, is a tiny mythical being that some people keep shut in a needle case in villages of the Pyrenees."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "minairo-c02",
      "source_id": "minairo-s1",
      "quote": "The minairons come from the perforate St John's-wort which blooms at the Saint John’s (the summer solstice) night. It grows in very deep caves guarded by giants and dragons and it only can be caught in the point of midnight on St. John’s Day.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Minairons berasal dari tumbuhan St John's-wort yang mekar pada malam Santo Yohanes (titik balik matahari musim panas), tumbuh di gua sangat dalam yang dijaga raksasa dan naga, dan hanya dapat diambil tepat tengah malam hari Santo Yohanes.",
        "en": "Minairons come from St John's-wort, which blooms on St John's Night (the summer solstice), grows in very deep caves guarded by giants and dragons, and can only be taken at midnight on St John's Day."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "minairo-c03",
      "source_id": "minairo-s1",
      "quote": "Others say that they are as a swarm of tiny mosquitoes, almost invisible. Some people prefer to compare them with the bee, because of the noise they do buzzing when left the needles’ tube or cane.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian orang menyebut mereka seperti kawanan nyamuk kecil yang hampir tak terlihat, yang lain membandingkannya dengan lebah karena dengungnya saat keluar dari tabung jarum.",
        "en": "Some say they are like a swarm of tiny, almost invisible mosquitoes; others compare them to bees for the buzzing they make leaving the needle case."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "minairo-c04",
      "source_id": "minairo-s1",
      "quote": "No one doubts, however, that they are so tiny that inside the cane there must be thousands and when one foregrounds, eager demands for work, saying, \"What will we do? What will we say?”",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka begitu kecil sehingga ribuan muat dalam tabung, dan begitu keluar mereka menuntut pekerjaan sambil berkata, \"Apa yang kita kerjakan? Apa yang kita katakan?\"",
        "en": "They are so tiny that thousands fit in the case, and when they come out they demand work, saying, 'What shall we do? What shall we say?'"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "minairo-c05",
      "source_id": "minairo-s1",
      "quote": "The legend says that many of the screes of the Pyrenees have been works of these goblins, when his master opened the needle tube by mistake or accident",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda menyebut banyak hamparan batu longsoran di Pyrenees adalah buatan mereka ketika tuannya tidak sengaja membuka tabung jarum.",
        "en": "Legend says many of the Pyrenean screes are their work, made when a master opened the needle case by mistake."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "minairo-c06",
      "source_id": "minairo-s1",
      "quote": "A handful of heirs of the Pyrenees main houses have been, on occasion, owners of the cane, through which the house went up like a mushroom.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejumlah pewaris rumah besar di Pyrenees konon memiliki tabung itu, sehingga rumah mereka makmur dengan cepat.",
        "en": "A few heirs of great Pyrenean houses reputedly owned the case, through which their house rose like a mushroom."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "minairo-c07",
      "source_id": "minairo-s3",
      "quote": "Al abrirse el canuto, los minairones salían y decían: què farem?, què direm? -\"¿qué haremos, qué diremos?\"- y si la persona que lo había abierto no les ordenaba hacer algún trabajo inmediatamente, lo mataban",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, bila tabung dibuka dan pembukanya tidak segera memberi pekerjaan, minairons membunuhnya.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article, when the case was opened, if the opener did not set them work at once, the minairons killed him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "minairo-c08",
      "source_id": "minairo-s2",
      "quote": "Mamarro ou les mamarroak sont des Ireluak ou génies de petite taille issus de la mythologie basque, ils aident les gens dans leurs tâches quotidiennes et protègent la famille dans laquelle ils vivent.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, padanan Basque-nya, mamarro atau mamarroak, adalah roh kecil yang membantu pekerjaan sehari-hari dan melindungi keluarga tempat mereka tinggal.",
        "en": "According to the French article, the Basque counterpart, mamarro or mamarroak, are small spirits who help with daily tasks and protect the family they live with."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "minairo-c09",
      "source_id": "minairo-s2",
      "quote": "Ces petits elfes portent les noms de Galtzagorriak, Prakagorriak, Famerijaleak à Kortezubi, Beharreztanak à Orozko, Aidetikakoak à Sare, Patuak ou Patuek à Ibarruri, Zaingorriak à Ordizia",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peri kecil ini juga disebut Galtzagorriak, Prakagorriak, dan Zaingorriak di Ordizia, di antara nama lain.",
        "en": "These little elves are also called Galtzagorriak, Prakagorriak and Zaingorriak at Ordizia, among other names."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Odei atau Hodei adalah roh guntur dan awan badai mitologi Basque yang merusak panen dengan petir dan kadang disebut putra jahat Mari.",
    "en": "Odei or Hodei is the Basque spirit of thunder and storm clouds who ruins crops with lightning and is sometimes called Mari's wicked son.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "odei-c01",
      "odei-c05",
      "odei-c06"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Odei, Hodei, atau Odai adalah roh guntur dan personifikasi awan badai dalam mitologi Basque, \"dia yang bergemuruh\". Ia menakuti petani dengan merusak panen lewat petir, tampak sebagai awan badai, dan dianggap pengganti dewa langit lama Ortzi; kadang ia putra jahat Mari. Saat guntur terdengar orang berkata \"Odeiak jo\", dan di Arrasate angin tenggara pembawa badai disebut angin Odei.",
      "en": "Odei, Hodei or Odai is the Basque spirit of thunder and personified storm cloud, 'the one who thunders'. He frightens farmers by ruining crops with lightning, appears as a storm cloud, and is seen as successor to the old sky god Ortzi; sometimes he is Mari's wicked son. At thunder people say 'Odeiak jo', and at Arrasate the stormy south-east wind is Odei's wind.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "odei-c01",
        "odei-c02",
        "odei-c03",
        "odei-c04",
        "odei-c05",
        "odei-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Untuk meredakan amarahnya, di Lapurdi orang menyalakan lilin yang diberkati, membakar laurel, atau menaruh kapak bermata ke atas di ambang pintu.",
      "en": "To calm his fury, in Labourd people light blessed candles, burn laurel or set an axe edge-up on the doorstep.",

        "odei-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "odei-c01",
      "source_id": "odei-s1",
      "quote": "In Basque mythology, Odei, also known as Hodei, is a spirit of thunder and the personification of storm clouds.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Basque, Odei atau Hodei adalah roh guntur dan personifikasi awan badai.",
        "en": "In Basque mythology Odei, also Hodei, is a spirit of thunder and the personification of storm clouds."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "odei-c02",
      "source_id": "odei-s2",
      "quote": "Odei, Hodei ou Odai est le nom dans la mythologie basque que l'on donne parfois pour désigner le tonnerre. D'autres appellent ainsi le gros nuage de tempête ou plus simplement l'agent du tonnerre, celui qui tonne.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Odei, Hodei, atau Odai adalah nama yang kadang dipakai untuk guntur, untuk awan badai besar, atau untuk \"dia yang bergemuruh\".",
        "en": "Odei, Hodei or Odai is a name sometimes given to thunder, to the great storm cloud, or to 'the one who thunders'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "odei-c03",
      "source_id": "odei-s2",
      "quote": "Quand on entend tonner on dit : « Odeiak jo » (« celui qui tonne a frappé »).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Saat terdengar guntur orang berkata \"Odeiak jo\", \"yang bergemuruh telah memukul\".",
        "en": "On hearing thunder people say 'Odeiak jo', 'the thunderer has struck'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "odei-c04",
      "source_id": "odei-s2",
      "quote": "Au quartier de Gesalibar, Arrasate (Guipuscoa) on appelle Odeiaixe « vent d'Odei » ou « vent du tonnerre », est un vent du sud-est qui apporte des tempêtes et des bourrasques.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Gesalibar, Arrasate (Gipuzkoa), angin tenggara pembawa badai disebut Odeiaixe, \"angin Odei\".",
        "en": "At Gesalibar in Arrasate (Gipuzkoa) the south-east wind that brings storms is called Odeiaixe, 'Odei's wind'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "odei-c05",
      "source_id": "odei-s3",
      "quote": "Atemorizaba a los campesinos echándoles a perder sus cosechas con sus rayos. Según los relatos, se muestra en forma de nube de tormenta, siendo el genio o dios de los cielos. Fue el sustituto del antiguo dios Ortzi o Urtzi.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, ia menakuti petani dengan merusak panen lewat petirnya, tampak sebagai awan badai, roh atau dewa langit, pengganti dewa lama Ortzi atau Urtzi.",
        "en": "According to the Spanish article he frightened farmers by ruining their crops with lightning, appeared as a storm cloud, the spirit or god of the skies, successor to the old god Ortzi or Urtzi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "odei-c06",
      "source_id": "odei-s3",
      "quote": "Ocasionalmente, aparece en las leyendas como hijo malvado de Mari, jefa de los genios.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadang dalam legenda ia muncul sebagai putra jahat Mari, pemimpin para roh.",
        "en": "In legends he sometimes appears as the wicked son of Mari, chief of the spirits."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "odei-c07",
      "source_id": "odei-s3",
      "quote": "En Saint-Esteben (Labort) se encienden velas bendecidas, se queman ramas de laurel en la casa o se sitúa un hacha en el umbral de la puerta principal de la casa, con el filo hacia arriba, para conjurarlo y aplacar su furia.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Saint-Esteben (Lapurdi) orang menyalakan lilin yang diberkati, membakar ranting laurel, atau menaruh kapak dengan mata menghadap ke atas di ambang pintu untuk meredakan amarahnya.",
        "en": "At Saint-Esteben (Labourd) people light blessed candles, burn laurel twigs or set an axe edge-up on the doorstep to ward him off and calm his fury."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia en, fr, dan es; belum ada sumber di luar Wikipedia yang dikutip untuk entri ini."

  ]
}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Pricolici adalah vampir sekaligus manusia serigala Rumania, orang jahat yang setelah mati bangkit berwujud serigala atau anjing untuk terus mencelakai manusia.",
    "en": "The pricolici is a Romanian vampire-werewolf, a wicked person who after death rises as a wolf or dog to keep harming people.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "pricolici-c01",
      "pricolici-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Pricolici adalah vampir Rumania bercirikan manusia serigala yang selalu mirip serigala atau anjing, berbeda dari strigoi yang tetap mirip manusia. Lelaki jahat dan kejam dikatakan menjadi pricolici setelah mati; menurut sebagian cerita, mereka manusia serigala semasa hidup dan vampir setelah mati, dan ada pula yang berasal dari anak yang disusui lagi setelah disapih. Artikel bahasa Rusia juga memakai nama ini untuk orang hidup yang sementara dapat menjadi serigala.",
      "en": "The pricolici is a Romanian vampire with werewolf traits that always looks like a wolf or dog, unlike the human-looking strigoi. Wicked, violent men are said to become pricolici after death; some tales make them werewolves in life and vampires after death, and others say they come from a child breastfed after weaning. The Russian article also applies the name to living people who can turn into wolves for a while.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "pricolici-c04",

    {
      "id": "Sampai zaman modern, warga pedesaan Rumania mengaku diserang serigala raksasa yang menyerang diam-diam orang yang sendirian dan menganggapnya pricolici. Namanya mungkin dari bahasa Latin lycus, \"serigala\".",
      "en": "Into modern times rural Romanians have claimed attacks by huge wolves striking lone victims silently, which they took for pricolici. Its name may come from Latin lycus, 'wolf'.",
