      "id": "cheval-gauvin-c04",
      "source_id": "cheval-gauvin-s1",
      "quote": "A harbinger of death, the Gauvin horse seems to have acted as a bogeyman for children. Perhaps the result of a goblin's transformation (a hobgoblin), he joins a large number of legendary Jura horses.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagai pertanda kematian, kuda Gauvin tampaknya juga momok penakut anak; mungkin jelmaan peri usil, ia termasuk banyak kuda legendaris Jura.",
        "en": "A harbinger of death, the Gauvin horse seems to have served as a children's bogeyman; perhaps a transformed goblin, it is one of many legendary Jura horses."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cheval-gauvin-c05",
      "source_id": "cheval-gauvin-s1",
      "quote": "The Gauvin horse bears the same name as a famous knight of the Round Table, nephew of King Arthur.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya sama dengan Gauvain, ksatria Meja Bundar dan keponakan Raja Arthur.",
        "en": "It shares its name with Gawain, the famous Round Table knight and nephew of King Arthur."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cheval-gauvin-c06",
      "source_id": "cheval-gauvin-s2",
      "quote": "Le témoignage d'une femme disant l'avoir rencontré dans le cimetière de Chamblay est commenté et conté depuis le XIXe siècle.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, kesaksian seorang perempuan yang mengaku bertemu kuda ini di kuburan Chamblay diceritakan dan dibahas sejak abad ke-19.",
        "en": "According to the French article, a woman's account of meeting it in the Chamblay cemetery has been told and discussed since the 19th century."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cheval-gauvin-c07",
      "source_id": "cheval-gauvin-s2",
      "quote": "L'illustrateur Jean-Louis Thouard l'a mis en images en 1996.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ilustrator Jean-Louis Thouard menggambarkannya pada 1996.",
        "en": "The illustrator Jean-Louis Thouard pictured it in 1996."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cheval-gauvin-c08",
      "source_id": "cheval-gauvin-s3",
      "quote": "El caballo Gauvin, caballo Gauvain, chevau Gauvin en el dialecto del jura o tcheva Gâvïn en franco-comtés, es un caballo legendario y maléfico propio de la región francesa del Franco Condado y del Macizo del Jura suizo.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Spanyol juga menyebut kuda Gauvin, chevau Gauvin dalam dialek Jura atau tcheva Gâvïn dalam bahasa Franche-Comté, kuda legendaris jahat dari Franche-Comté dan Jura Swiss.",
        "en": "The Spanish article also calls the Gauvin horse, chevau Gauvin in Jura dialect or tcheva Gâvïn in Franc-Comtois, a legendary evil horse of Franche-Comté and the Swiss Jura."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Cheval Mallet adalah kuda jahat dari Vendée dan Pays de Retz yang muncul pada malam hari, menggoda pelancong lelah untuk menungganginya, lalu membawa mereka pergi selamanya.",
    "en": "The Cheval Mallet is an evil horse of the Vendée and Pays de Retz that appears at night, tempts weary travellers to ride it, and carries them off for good.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "cheval-mallet-c01",
      "cheval-mallet-c02"

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Cheval Mallet adalah kuda dongeng yang jahat dari Vendée, Poitou, dan Pays de Retz. Pada malam hari ia muncul sebagai kuda putih atau hitam lengkap dengan pelana, menggoda pelancong yang lelah; yang naik tidak pernah kembali kecuali membawa ongkos atau medali Santo Benediktus. Ia dianggap alat Iblis, mungkin terkait Sleipnir dan Perburuan Liar, dan mirip Lou Drapé.",
      "en": "The Cheval Mallet is a fabulous evil horse of the Vendée, Poitou and the Pays de Retz. At night it appears as a saddled white or black horse tempting weary travellers; riders never return unless they carry the fare or a St Benedict medal. It is seen as the Devil's tool, perhaps linked to Sleipnir and the Wild Hunt, and resembles Lou Drapé.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cheval-mallet-c01",
        "cheval-mallet-c02",
        "cheval-mallet-c04",
        "cheval-mallet-c06"

    },
    {
      "id": "Namanya mungkin dari kata Kelt untuk peti. Di Saint-Lumine-de-Coutais pernah ada pesta Kuda Merlet dengan pemeran berkostum kuda di sekitar pohon ek, yang dilarang pada 1791.",
      "en": "Its name may come from a Celtic word for a trunk. Saint-Lumine-de-Coutais once held a Horse Merlet feast with an actor dressed as a horse around an oak, banned in 1791.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cheval-mallet-c03",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "cheval-mallet-c01",
      "source_id": "cheval-mallet-s1",
      "quote": "The Cheval Mallet (or Malet, meaning Mallet Horse) describes a fabulous and evil horse mentioned in folklore around the French Vendée, Poitou, and more frequently in the Pays de Retz, near Lac de Grand Lieu.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cheval Mallet atau Malet adalah kuda dongeng yang jahat dalam cerita rakyat Vendée, Poitou, dan terutama Pays de Retz dekat Danau Grand-Lieu, Prancis.",
        "en": "The Cheval Mallet or Malet is a fabulous evil horse of the folklore of the Vendée, Poitou and especially the Pays de Retz near Lac de Grand-Lieu, France."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cheval-mallet-c02",
      "source_id": "cheval-mallet-s1",
      "quote": "It was supposed to appear at night (sometimes specifically in the middle of the night) as a beautiful white or black horse, saddled and bridled, and tempt travelers who were exhausted from a long journey. Unwary travelers who accepted a ride would never be seen again, unless they had the price of travel or a protection spell, such as a medal of St. Benedict.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia muncul pada malam hari sebagai kuda putih atau hitam yang indah, lengkap dengan pelana dan tali kekang, menggoda pelancong yang kelelahan; yang naik tidak pernah terlihat lagi kecuali membawa ongkos perjalanan atau jimat seperti medali Santo Benediktus.",
        "en": "It appeared at night as a beautiful white or black horse, saddled and bridled, tempting exhausted travellers; those who rode it were never seen again unless they carried the fare or a protective charm such as a St Benedict medal."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cheval-mallet-c03",
      "source_id": "cheval-mallet-s1",
      "quote": "There was also a feast known as Horse Merlette, Merlet or Mallet in the town of Saint-Lumine-de-Coutais. It was a celebration of renewal and featured several actors around one oak, one disguised as a horse. It was opposed by the ecclesiastical authorities and banned in 1791.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Saint-Lumine-de-Coutais ada pesta Kuda Merlette, Merlet, atau Mallet, perayaan pembaruan dengan para pemeran di sekitar pohon ek, salah satunya berkostum kuda; pesta itu ditentang gereja dan dilarang pada 1791.",
        "en": "Saint-Lumine-de-Coutais held a feast of the Horse Merlette, Merlet or Mallet, a renewal festival with actors around an oak, one dressed as a horse; the Church opposed it and it was banned in 1791."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cheval-mallet-c04",
      "source_id": "cheval-mallet-s2",
      "quote": "Le cheval Mallet est vu comme un instrument du Diable, voire une forme de Satan lui-même. Peut-être issu de Sleipnir et de la chasse sauvage, sa légende est très semblable à celle d'autres chevaux fabuleux tels que lou drapé ou la blanque jument.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, ia dianggap alat Iblis atau wujud Setan sendiri, mungkin berasal dari Sleipnir dan Perburuan Liar, dan legendanya mirip kuda dongeng lain seperti Lou Drapé dan blanque jument.",
        "en": "According to the French article it is seen as the Devil's instrument or Satan himself, perhaps derived from Sleipnir and the Wild Hunt, and its legend is like those of other fabulous horses such as Lou Drapé or the blanque jument."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cheval-mallet-c05",
      "source_id": "cheval-mallet-s2",
      "quote": "Le nom de « Mallet », et par corruption « Merlet », pourrait être issu de mail qui signifie « malle » ou « valise » dans les anciennes langues celtiques.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Mallet, yang berubah menjadi Merlet, mungkin dari kata mail, \"peti\" atau \"koper\", dalam bahasa Kelt kuno.",
        "en": "The name Mallet, corrupted to Merlet, may come from mail, 'trunk' or 'case', in old Celtic languages."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cheval-mallet-c06",
      "source_id": "cheval-mallet-s3",
      "quote": "El Caballo Martillo (también llamado caballo mazo o caballo Malet; del francés Cheval Mallet o Cheval Malet) es un caballo mítico y un ente malvado que se menciona en el folclore francés de Vendée",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Spanyol juga menyebut Cheval Mallet kuda mitis dan makhluk jahat dalam cerita rakyat Vendée.",
        "en": "The Spanish article likewise calls the Cheval Mallet a mythical horse and evil being of Vendée folklore."
      }
    }
  ],

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Duberdicus adalah nama dewa Lusitania kuno dari wilayah Portugal kini, dahulu dianggap dewa air tetapi oleh Jorge de Alarcão dikaitkan dengan sebuah benteng.",
    "en": "Duberdicus is an ancient Lusitanian theonym from what is now Portugal, long thought a water god but tied by Jorge de Alarcão to a fortress.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "duberdicus-c01",
      "duberdicus-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Duberdicus atau Duberdico adalah nama dewa dari budaya Lusitania kuno di wilayah Portugal kini. Ia lama ditafsirkan sebagai dewa air, mata air, atau sungai, tetapi sejarawan Jorge de Alarcão menolak tafsiran itu dan menganggapnya nama dewa yang terkait benteng atau wilayah tertentu.",
      "en": "Duberdicus or Duberdico is a theonym of ancient Lusitanian culture in what is now Portugal. It was long read as a god of water, springs or rivers, but the historian Jorge de Alarcão rejects that reading and takes it for a theonym tied to a particular fortress or territory.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "duberdicus-c01",
        "duberdicus-c02",
        "duberdicus-c03",
        "duberdicus-c04",
        "duberdicus-c05"
      ]
    }

      "title": "Ντουμπερντίκους",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "duberdicus-c01",
      "source_id": "duberdicus-s1",
      "quote": "Duberdicus or Duberdico, is a Lusitanian theonym, in the cultural area of Lusitania (in the territory of modern Portugal).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Duberdicus atau Duberdico adalah nama dewa Lusitania dari wilayah budaya Lusitania, kini Portugal.",
        "en": "Duberdicus or Duberdico is a Lusitanian theonym from the cultural area of Lusitania, in modern Portugal."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "duberdicus-c02",
      "source_id": "duberdicus-s1",
      "quote": "It has historically been assumed that this was a water god, but Jorge de Alarcão disagrees with this assertion (as well as several other alleged cases of water deities in the Lusitanian sphere). He assumes that it is a theonym associated with a specific fortress.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dahulu ia dianggap dewa air, tetapi Jorge de Alarcão tidak setuju dan menganggapnya nama dewa yang terkait benteng tertentu.",
        "en": "It has long been assumed a water god, but Jorge de Alarcão disagrees and takes it for a theonym tied to a particular fortress."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "duberdicus-c03",
      "source_id": "duberdicus-s2",
      "quote": "Tradicionalmente, tem sido interpretado como uma divindade aquática, possivelmente associada a rios ou fontes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Portugis, ia secara tradisional ditafsirkan sebagai dewa air, mungkin terkait sungai atau mata air.",
        "en": "According to the Portuguese article, it has traditionally been read as a water deity, perhaps tied to rivers or springs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "duberdicus-c04",
      "source_id": "duberdicus-s2",
      "quote": "Segundo o autor, Duberdicus seria antes um teónimo associado a uma fortificação específica ou a um espaço territorial definido.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Alarcão, Duberdicus lebih mungkin nama dewa yang terkait benteng atau wilayah tertentu.",
        "en": "According to Alarcão, Duberdicus is rather a theonym tied to a particular fortification or defined territory."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "duberdicus-c05",
      "source_id": "duberdicus-s3",
      "quote": "Ο Ντουμπερντίκους (Πορτογαλικά:Duberdicus) ήταν ο θεός των πηγών και του νερού στη Λουζιτανική μυθολογία.",
      "locator": null,

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Follet adalah roh rumah kecil yang usil dalam mitologi Katalan dan Aragon, setara dengan folletto Italia dan lutin Prancis, yang masuk ke rumah pada malam hari.",
    "en": "The follet is a mischievous little house spirit of Catalan and Aragonese mythology, akin to the Italian folletto and French lutin, that slips into homes at night.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "follet-c01",
      "follet-c02",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Follet, dari kata foll, \"gila\", adalah roh usil mitologi Katalan, juga disebut fullet atau millet; dalam mitologi Aragon ia makhluk kecil yang masuk ke rumah pada malam hari untuk mencuri barang kesukaannya. Ia setara dengan brownie, duende, folletto, lutin, dan gripet, dan di wilayah Katalan peran serupa diisi \"familiar\" seperti cerdet.",
      "en": "The follet, from foll, 'mad', is a mischievous spirit of Catalan mythology, also called fullet or millet; in Aragonese mythology it is a tiny being that enters homes at night to steal what it likes. It is akin to the brownie, duende, folletto, lutin and gripet, and in the Catalan lands 'familiars' such as the cerdet play a similar role.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "follet-c01",
        "follet-c02",
        "follet-c03",
        "follet-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam bahasa Italia folletto adalah nama umum untuk makhluk gaib kecil yang aktif pada malam hari, usil, dan terkait rumah dan kandang, yang bisa membantu atau mengganggu; namanya merujuk sifatnya yang tak menentu. Para peneliti menelusurinya ke Lares dan Penates Romawi.",
      "en": "In Italian, folletto is a generic name for tiny nocturnal, mischievous supernatural beings tied to house and stable, who may help or harass; the name points to their fickleness. Scholars trace them to the Roman Lares and Penates.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "follet-c05",
        "follet-c06",
        "follet-c07"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "follet-c01",
      "source_id": "follet-s3",
      "quote": "Un follet (derivat de foll; dit fullet a Mallorca), dit també millet, és un esperit fantàstic de la mitologia catalana de caràcter entremaliat.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Follet (dari kata foll, \"gila\"; di Mallorca disebut fullet), juga millet, adalah roh khayalan mitologi Katalan yang usil.",
        "en": "The follet (from foll, 'mad'; fullet in Mallorca), also millet, is a mischievous fantastic spirit of Catalan mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "follet-c02",
      "source_id": "follet-s3",
      "quote": "Si fa no fa, és anàleg al gnom de la literatura europea, al goblin o el hobgoblin del folklore europeu, si més no en la seva cara més amable; també es pot equiparar al brownie (brùnaidh) escocès, al duende castellà, al folletto italià, al lutin francès i als occitans gripet, trufandèc, folet i fadet.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia kurang lebih setara dengan gnome, goblin, atau hobgoblin Eropa dalam sisi ramahnya, serta brownie Skotlandia, duende Kastilia, folletto Italia, lutin Prancis, dan gripet atau fadet Oksitan.",
        "en": "It is roughly like the European gnome, goblin or hobgoblin in their kinder aspect, and comparable to the Scottish brownie, the Castilian duende, the Italian folletto, the French lutin and the Occitan gripet or fadet."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "follet-c03",
      "source_id": "follet-s3",
      "quote": "Dins el mateix territori català, la funció del follet també l'ocupen altres criatures, sovint anomenades «familiars», com ara el cerdet a Alacant",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di wilayah Katalan sendiri peran follet juga diisi makhluk lain yang disebut \"familiar\", seperti cerdet di Alicante.",
        "en": "Within the Catalan lands the follet's role is also filled by other creatures, often called 'familiars', such as the cerdet in Alicante."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "follet-c04",
      "source_id": "follet-s2",
      "quote": "Un follet ye un ser chicorrón d'a mitolochía aragonesa que dentra en os fogars per as nueiz pa furtar lo que mas le fa goyo.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Aragon, follet adalah makhluk kecil mitologi Aragon yang masuk ke rumah pada malam hari untuk mencuri apa yang paling disukainya.",
        "en": "According to the Aragonese article, a follet is a tiny being of Aragonese mythology that enters homes at night to steal what it likes best."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "follet-c05",
      "source_id": "follet-s1",
      "quote": "Folletto è il nome con cui la lingua italiana designa genericamente una vasta categoria di esseri soprannaturali minori del folclore europeo: creature di piccola statura, notturne, dispettose e mutevoli, legate alla casa, alla stalla e alla campagna, capaci di rendere servizi agli uomini quanto di perseguitarli.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Italia, folletto adalah nama umum untuk banyak makhluk gaib kecil dalam cerita rakyat Eropa: bertubuh mungil, aktif pada malam hari, usil, dan berubah-ubah, terkait rumah, kandang, dan ladang, yang bisa membantu maupun mengganggu manusia.",
        "en": "According to the Italian article, folletto is the generic Italian name for a broad class of minor supernatural beings of European folklore: tiny, nocturnal, mischievous and changeable, tied to house, stable and field, able to help or to plague humans."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "follet-c06",
      "source_id": "follet-s1",
      "quote": "Folletto è propriamente il diminutivo di folle, e allude all'incostanza e all'imprevedibilità attribuite alla creatura. La stessa motivazione è alla base del francese follet e del provenzale fadet: dal Medioevo il lutin francese è chiamato anche follet, cioè «piccolo folle», per i suoi sbalzi d'umore",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Folletto adalah bentuk kecil dari folle, \"gila\", merujuk sifat makhluk ini yang tak menentu; dasar yang sama ada pada kata Prancis follet dan Provençal fadet, dan sejak Abad Pertengahan lutin Prancis juga disebut follet, \"si gila kecil\".",
        "en": "Folletto is the diminutive of folle, 'mad', alluding to the creature's fickleness; the same idea lies behind French follet and Provençal fadet, and since the Middle Ages the French lutin has also been called follet, 'little madman'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "follet-c07",
      "source_id": "follet-s1",
      "quote": "La storiografia demologica riconduce la figura ai geni domestici del mondo antico — i Lari e i Penati romani — sopravvissuti alla cristianizzazione in forma clandestina e degradata",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para peneliti tradisi rakyat menelusuri tokoh ini ke roh rumah dunia kuno, Lares dan Penates Romawi, yang bertahan setelah kristenisasi dalam bentuk tersembunyi dan merosot.",
        "en": "Folklore scholarship traces the figure to the household spirits of antiquity, the Roman Lares and Penates, which survived Christianisation in hidden, degraded form."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Gaizkiñ adalah roh jahat Basque penyebab penyakit yang terbentuk dari isi bantal berupa kepala ayam jantan dan membuat anak yang tidur jatuh sakit.",
    "en": "Gaizkiñ is a disease-causing Basque evil spirit that forms in pillow stuffing as a rooster's head and makes sleeping children ill.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "gaizkin-c01",
      "gaizkin-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Gaizkiñ, \"yang jahat\", adalah irelu atau roh malam penyebab penyakit dalam mitologi Basque. Ia bersembunyi di bawah bantal dan terbentuk dari bulu atau wol isinya sebagai kepala ayam jantan yang buruk rupa dan sulit dilihat, lalu membuat anak yang tidur di ranjang itu sakit secara misterius atau bahkan mati. Untuk menyembuhkannya, kepala ayam itu harus dibakar di perapian.",
      "en": "Gaizkiñ, 'the wicked one', is an irelu or night spirit of disease in Basque mythology. It hides under the pillow and forms in the feather or wool stuffing as a hideous, hard-to-see rooster's head, giving the child sleeping there a mysterious illness or even death. To cure it, the rooster's head must be burned in the hearth.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gaizkin-c01",
        "gaizkin-c02",
        "gaizkin-c03",
        "gaizkin-c04",
        "gaizkin-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Di sebagian tempat roh familiar mamurrak juga disebut gaizkin, dan ia dikaitkan dengan Inguma dan roh cuaca buruk Aide-gaizto.",
      "en": "In places the familiar spirits called mamurrak are also called gaizkin, and it is linked with Inguma and the bad-weather spirit Aide-gaizto.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gaizkin-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "gaizkin-c01",
      "source_id": "gaizkin-s1",
      "quote": "In Basque mythology, Gaizkiñ is an evil spirit that causes diseases.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Basque, Gaizkiñ adalah roh jahat penyebab penyakit.",
        "en": "In Basque mythology Gaizkiñ is an evil spirit that causes diseases."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gaizkin-c02",
      "source_id": "gaizkin-s2",
      "quote": "Gaizkiña, Gaizkiñe ou Gaizkina  est un méchant Irelu ou génie nocturne qui provoque des maladies dans la mythologie basque. Il peut se cacher sous l'oreiller (buruko), y manipule la laine afin de provoquer des maladies ou la mort.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, Gaizkiña adalah irelu atau roh malam jahat yang bersembunyi di bawah bantal dan mengutak-atik wolnya untuk menimbulkan penyakit atau kematian.",
        "en": "According to the French article, Gaizkiña is a wicked irelu or night spirit that hides under the pillow and works the wool to cause sickness or death."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gaizkin-c03",
      "source_id": "gaizkin-s2",
      "quote": "Gaizkin signifie « malveillant, pervers, délinquant » en basque.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gaizkin berarti \"jahat, bejat, durjana\" dalam bahasa Basque.",
        "en": "Gaizkin means 'malevolent, perverse, wicked' in Basque."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gaizkin-c04",
      "source_id": "gaizkin-s2",
      "quote": "Gaizkin est une créature criminelle qui prend forme dans les plumes ou la laine utilisées pour remplir l'intérieur des oreillers et des traversins, sous la forme d'une tête de coq. Hideux, il n'est pas facile à sentir et à voir. Il provoque alors une maladie mystérieuse à l'enfant qui dort dans le lit.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gaizkin terbentuk dari bulu atau wol isi bantal dan guling berupa kepala ayam jantan, buruk rupa dan sulit dirasakan atau dilihat, dan membuat anak yang tidur di ranjang itu sakit secara misterius.",
        "en": "Gaizkin takes shape in the feathers or wool stuffing pillows and bolsters as a rooster's head, hideous and hard to sense or see, and gives the child sleeping in the bed a mysterious illness."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gaizkin-c05",
      "source_id": "gaizkin-s2",
      "quote": "Pour guérir les malades, la tête du coq ou l'image du coq, qui est l'esprit du Malin, doit être brûlée dans le feu de la maison.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Untuk menyembuhkan si sakit, kepala atau gambar ayam jantan itu, roh si Jahat, harus dibakar di perapian rumah.",
        "en": "To cure the sick, the rooster's head or image, the spirit of the Evil One, must be burned in the household fire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gaizkin-c06",
      "source_id": "gaizkin-s3",
      "quote": "En alguns llocs, els genis familiars coneguts com a mamurrak s'anomenen també gaizkin. També es diu que el geni malèfic associat al mal temps, Aide-gaizto, està relacionat amb alguns esperits malèvols de naturalesa domèstica, com ara Inguma o Gaizkin.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Katalan, di sebagian tempat roh familiar mamurrak juga disebut gaizkin, dan roh cuaca buruk Aide-gaizto dikatakan berkaitan dengan roh rumah yang jahat seperti Inguma dan Gaizkin.",
        "en": "According to the Catalan article, in places the familiar spirits called mamurrak are also called gaizkin, and Aide-gaizto, the evil spirit of bad weather, is said to be linked with wicked household spirits such as Inguma and Gaizkin."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

    "id": "Gizotso adalah manusia serigala mitologi Basque, monster setengah manusia setengah serigala yang tinggal di sekitar hutan dan muncul pada malam hari berbalut rantai.",
    "en": "Gizotso is the werewolf of Basque mythology, a half-man, half-wolf monster living near the woods and appearing at night laden with chains.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "gizotso-c01",
      "gizotso-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Gizotso, dari gizon \"manusia\" dan otso \"serigala\", adalah manusia serigala Basque: monster setengah manusia setengah serigala yang tinggal di sekitar hutan dan muncul pada malam hari dengan rantai. Di Luzaide ia dipercaya sangat kuat, dan di Agiano dekat Zeanuri ia dianggap lahir dari hubungan binatang dan perempuan; di sana ia konon menyerang seorang perempuan yang bergegas pulang.",
      "en": "Gizotso, from gizon 'man' and otso 'wolf', is the Basque werewolf: a half-man, half-wolf monster living around the woods and appearing at night in chains. At Luzaide it is thought immensely strong, and at Agiano near Zeanuri it is held to be born of a beast and a woman; there it reportedly attacked a woman hurrying home.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gizotso-c01",
        "gizotso-c02",
        "gizotso-c04",
        "gizotso-c05",
        "gizotso-c06",
        "gizotso-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sumber berbeda soal luka perempuan itu: payudaranya dirobek atau matanya dicungkil. Gizotso berbeda dari Otsoko, personifikasi serigala, dan Pierre de Lancre pada 1612 sudah menulis tentang \"Monsieur La Forêt\" yang meneror Pyrenees.",
      "en": "Sources differ on the woman's wounds: her breasts torn off or her eyes torn out. Gizotso is distinct from Otsoko, the personified wolf, and in 1612 Pierre de Lancre already wrote of a 'Monsieur La Forêt' haunting the Pyrenees.",
      "claim_ids": [

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "gizotso-c01",
      "source_id": "gizotso-s1",
      "quote": "Gizotso ou Gizotxo est le mot désignant en basque l'« Homme-loup » ou Loup-garou dans la mythologie basque.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gizotso atau Gizotxo adalah kata Basque untuk \"manusia serigala\" dalam mitologi Basque.",
        "en": "Gizotso or Gizotxo is the Basque word for the 'man-wolf' or werewolf in Basque mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gizotso-c02",
      "source_id": "gizotso-s1",
      "quote": "Gizotso signifie « homme-loup » en basque, contraction de gizon (« homme », ou bien peut-être de sa forme giza, utilisée dans les mots composés",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gizotso berarti \"manusia serigala\", gabungan gizon (\"manusia\") atau bentuk giza dengan otso (\"serigala\").",
        "en": "Gizotso means 'man-wolf', from gizon ('man') or its compound form giza plus otso ('wolf')."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gizotso-c03",
      "source_id": "gizotso-s1",
      "quote": "En 1612, Pierre de Rosteguy de Lancre écrit deux ans après le procès de sorcellerie un livre intitulé Tableau de l'inconstance des mauvais anges et démons. Il y évoque à plusieurs reprises l'existence d'un « Monsieur La Forêt » qui sévirait en Gascogne et dans les Pyrénées sous l'apparence d'un homme noir.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 1612, Pierre de Lancre menulis Tableau de l'inconstance des mauvais anges et démons, yang berulang kali menyebut \"Monsieur La Forêt\" yang meneror Gascogne dan Pyrenees berwujud lelaki hitam.",
        "en": "In 1612 Pierre de Lancre wrote the Tableau de l'inconstance des mauvais anges et démons, repeatedly mentioning a 'Monsieur La Forêt' who ravaged Gascony and the Pyrenees as a black man."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gizotso-c04",
      "source_id": "gizotso-s1",
      "quote": "C'est un être considéré comme un monstre, moitié-homme et moitié-loup. Il habite les alentours des bois et il apparaît parfois la nuit, chargés de chaînes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dianggap monster setengah manusia setengah serigala yang tinggal di sekitar hutan dan kadang muncul pada malam hari dengan membawa rantai.",
        "en": "It is considered a monster, half man and half wolf, living around the woods and sometimes appearing at night laden with chains."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gizotso-c05",
      "source_id": "gizotso-s1",
      "quote": "On raconte qu'un Gizotso habite dans l'obscurité du village pyrénéen de Luzaide, on imagine qu'il possède une force surhumaine.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Konon seorang Gizotso tinggal dalam kegelapan desa Pyrenees Luzaide dan memiliki kekuatan luar biasa.",
        "en": "A Gizotso is said to dwell in the darkness of the Pyrenean village of Luzaide, with superhuman strength."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gizotso-c06",
      "source_id": "gizotso-s1",
      "quote": "Dans la vallée d'Arratia, autour de Zeanuri, plus exactement dans un endroit appelé Agiano, non loin des mondes souterrains de Baltzola, les gens là-bas croient que le Gizotso est le fruit de l'union charnelle entre la Bête et une femme.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di lembah Arratia sekitar Zeanuri, di tempat bernama Agiano dekat gua Baltzola, orang percaya Gizotso lahir dari hubungan antara binatang dan seorang perempuan.",
        "en": "In the Arratia valley near Zeanuri, at a place called Agiano near the Baltzola caves, people believe the Gizotso is born of a union between a beast and a woman."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gizotso-c07",
      "source_id": "gizotso-s1",
      "quote": "La femme se dirigea chez elle mais le Gizotso la dépassa et lui arracha les seins.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah Agiano, seorang perempuan bergegas pulang, tetapi Gizotso menyusulnya dan merobek payudaranya.",
        "en": "In the Agiano tale a woman hurried home, but the Gizotso overtook her and tore off her breasts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gizotso-c08",
      "source_id": "gizotso-s2",
      "quote": "La gente dice que en el lugar llamado Agiano (Ceánuri) vivía una mujer que iba a su casa, pero el Gizotso se topó con ella y le arrancó los ojos.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Spanyol menyebut Gizotso mencungkil mata perempuan di Agiano.",
        "en": "The Spanish article says the Gizotso tore out the eyes of the woman at Agiano."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gizotso-c09",
      "source_id": "gizotso-s3",
      "quote": "No és el mateix personatge que l'Otsoko, que és la personificació del llop, sinó un altre d'equiparable als de les llegendes d'homes llop presents arreu d'Europa",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Katalan, ia bukan Otsoko, personifikasi serigala, melainkan setara dengan manusia serigala dalam legenda di seluruh Eropa.",
        "en": "According to the Catalan article it is not Otsoko, the personified wolf, but the equivalent of werewolves in legends all over Europe."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Hircocervus atau tragelaphos adalah makhluk khayalan setengah kambing setengah rusa yang dipakai Platon dan Aristoteles sebagai contoh sesuatu yang dapat dibayangkan tetapi tidak ada.",
    "en": "The hircocervus or tragelaph is an imaginary half-goat, half-stag that Plato and Aristotle used as an example of something imaginable but non-existent.",

    {
      "id": "Hircocervus, dari bahasa Latin hircus \"kambing jantan\" dan cervus \"rusa jantan\", atau tragelaphos dalam bahasa Yunani, adalah makhluk khayalan setengah kambing setengah rusa. Dalam Republik, Sokrates menyamakan pemikirannya dengan pelukis kambing-rusa, dan Aristoteles memakainya sebagai contoh hal yang dapat dijelaskan tetapi tidak ada; Boethius menerjemahkannya menjadi hircocervus. Diodorus Siculus justru menganggapnya hewan nyata.",
      "en": "The hircocervus, from Latin hircus 'billy goat' and cervus 'stag', or tragelaphos in Greek, is an imaginary half-goat, half-stag. In the Republic Socrates compares his thinking to painters of goat-stags, and Aristotle used it as an example of something describable yet non-existent; Boethius translated it as hircocervus. Diodorus Siculus, though, treated it as a real animal.",

      "en": "The word entered English in 1398, was used by Martin Luther in 1540 for a law that does not condemn, and later became a figure for anything absurd. Buffon identified Pliny's tragelaph as an old long-maned stag.",

  "claims": [

      "id": "hircocervus-c01",
      "source_id": "hircocervus-s1",
      "quote": "The hircocervus (Latin: hircus, \"billy goat\" + cervus, \"stag\") or tragelaph (Greek: τράγος, romanized: tragos, \"billy goat\" + έλαφος, elaphos, \"stag\"), also known as a goat-stag, was a legendary creature imagined to be half-goat, half-stag.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hircocervus (Latin: hircus, \"kambing jantan\" + cervus, \"rusa jantan\") atau tragelaphos (Yunani), juga disebut kambing-rusa, adalah makhluk legendaris yang dibayangkan setengah kambing setengah rusa.",
        "en": "The hircocervus (Latin hircus, 'billy goat' + cervus, 'stag') or tragelaph (Greek), also called goat-stag, was a legendary creature imagined as half goat, half stag."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hircocervus-c02",
      "source_id": "hircocervus-s1",
      "quote": "In Plato's Republic, Socrates speaks of his own image-making as similar to that of painters who paint goat-stags, combining the features of different things together (488a).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Republik karya Platon, Sokrates menyamakan caranya membuat gambaran dengan pelukis yang melukis kambing-rusa, menggabungkan ciri berbagai hal (488a).",
        "en": "In Plato's Republic, Socrates likens his image-making to painters who paint goat-stags by combining features of different things (488a)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hircocervus-c03",
      "source_id": "hircocervus-s1",
      "quote": "In his work De Interpretatione, Aristotle utilized the idea of a fabulous goat-stag to express the philosophical concept of something that is describable even though it does not really exist. His Greek \"τραγέλαφος\" was rendered into Latin \"hircocervus\" by Boethius.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam De Interpretatione, Aristoteles memakai kambing-rusa dongeng untuk menjelaskan konsep sesuatu yang dapat dijelaskan walau tidak benar-benar ada; Boethius menerjemahkan kata Yunaninya menjadi hircocervus.",
        "en": "In De Interpretatione Aristotle used the fabulous goat-stag to express something describable though non-existent; Boethius rendered his Greek word into Latin as hircocervus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hircocervus-c04",
      "source_id": "hircocervus-s1",
      "quote": "On the other hand, Diodorus Siculus treats the tragelaphos as an existing animal, and there are references in Greek literature to other hybrid creatures such as the hippelaphos (horse-stag).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebaliknya, Diodorus Siculus memperlakukan tragelaphos sebagai hewan yang benar-benar ada, dan sastra Yunani juga menyebut hibrida lain seperti hippelaphos (kuda-rusa).",
        "en": "Diodorus Siculus, by contrast, treats the tragelaphos as a real animal, and Greek literature mentions other hybrids such as the hippelaphos (horse-stag)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hircocervus-c05",
      "source_id": "hircocervus-s1",
      "quote": "The word hircocervus first appears in the English language in a medieval manuscript dating from 1398 (now at the Bodleian Library).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata hircocervus pertama muncul dalam bahasa Inggris pada naskah abad pertengahan tahun 1398 yang kini di Bodleian Library.",
        "en": "The word hircocervus first appears in English in a medieval manuscript of 1398, now in the Bodleian Library."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hircocervus-c06",
      "source_id": "hircocervus-s1",
      "quote": "Martin Luther uses the term \"tragelaphus\" in his Theses Against the Antinomians (1540, Sixth Set) to describe \"a law that does not condemn\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Martin Luther memakai istilah tragelaphus dalam Tesis Melawan Kaum Antinomian (1540) untuk menggambarkan \"hukum yang tidak menghukum\".",
        "en": "Martin Luther used 'tragelaphus' in his Theses Against the Antinomians (1540) for 'a law that does not condemn'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hircocervus-c07",
      "source_id": "hircocervus-s2",
      "quote": "Quant à Pline, il parle d'un tragélaphe qui est sans doute le même animal. Buffon a reconnu sous ces deux noms un vieux cerf dont le cou finit par se couvrir de longs crins et le fait ressembler de loin à un cheval.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Prancis, Plinius menyebut tragelaphos yang mungkin hewan yang sama dengan hippelaphos Aristoteles, dan Buffon mengenalinya sebagai rusa tua yang lehernya tertutup surai panjang sehingga dari jauh mirip kuda.",
        "en": "According to the French article, Pliny's tragelaph is probably the same as Aristotle's hippelaphos, and Buffon identified both as an old stag whose neck grows long hair so that from afar it looks like a horse."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hircocervus-c08",
      "source_id": "hircocervus-s3",
      "quote": "Col tempo l'uso letterale del termine è stato abbandonato in favore di quello metaforico per riferirsi a cose assurde ed irreali.",
